# Technical-debt register

Debt that isn't written down isn't managed. Managed by `/debt` (add/list/promote); agents append as they find it.
Refuse vague entries — record concrete Impact and Interest (does it get worse if ignored?).

> 🔴 **READ THIS BEFORE CITING A DEBT ID — this register contains DUPLICATE IDs, and they are different items (found 2026-09-22, Dunga triage).**
> The file has **two independent series**: the **table** below, and the **prose sections** further down. They were numbered independently and collided. **`DEBT-46` through `DEBT-56` each name TWO unrelated items.** (**`DEBT-45` is the benign case and the one that makes the rest look harmless** — table row and prose section are the *same* item, T-01.4.10 leg 1 / the gitleaks fingerprint, the prose being the detail of the row. Do not generalise from it.) Worked examples, so nobody has to re-derive it: table `DEBT-47` is *"the project static gate never type-checks `tests/`"* (Atchim S-01.4 batch-1) while prose `DEBT-47` is *"the declared-type lens is applied to the reported field"* (Zangado re-audit #4); table `DEBT-48` is `GOLDEN_DATASET_NAME` naming, prose `DEBT-48` is the nine-row fail-open sweep; table `DEBT-55`/`DEBT-56` are HARDEN-01 GAP-4/GAP-5 (**both closed**) while prose `DEBT-55`/`DEBT-56` are the rigor matrix (**open**) and the `python-dotenv` shim (**resolved**). **A cross-reference like "see DEBT-55" is therefore ambiguous, and one of the two readings is closed** — which is how a live item gets read as dead.
> **Rules from today, pending a proper renumber:** (1) **always cite as `DEBT-NN (table)` or `DEBT-NN (prose)`** when the number is in 45–56; (2) ~~**new IDs are allocated from `DEBT-69` upward** (max existing is `DEBT-68`, prose)~~ — **amended 2026-09-23 (Wave-0 audit): allocate from `DEBT-80` upward; the max is now `DEBT-79` (table), filed by that audit**; (3) **do NOT renumber retroactively** — dozens of cross-references across `REGRESSIONS.md`, `PROGRESS.md`, `HANDOFFS.md`, `SPEC-01` and the QA reports point at these ids, and silently re-pointing them is a worse failure than the collision. A deliberate renumber with a mapping table is a carded piece of work, not a tidy-up.
> **Why it happened, for the record:** the prose series was appended by the re-audit/HARDEN lanes on 2026-09-21 while the table was being appended by the S-01.4 review lanes the same day — collision-safe ID allocation (`max + 1`) was applied per *section* rather than per *file*. Same shape as every other item in the DEBT-40/43/47 family: **the instrument's scope was narrower than the class it was meant to cover.**
> ➡️ **Dispositions of the six open process rows and the 2026-09-22 intake are inline on each item, marked `⚖️ DISPOSITION 2026-09-22`.**

## ✅ LANES D & F — closed 2026-09-25, independently reviewed and QA'd

Implementer: this session (Opus 5). Reviewers: Atchim (sonnet) and Zangado (fable) — distinct
model instances from the implementer and from each other, per **DEBT-44** and the `## Rigor`
matrix's `Risk: high` row, which reads from the SPEC header and not from the profile.

| Row | Outcome |
|---|---|
| **DEBT-83** | ✅ **CLOSED.** Duplicate `document_id` refused in `_validate_dataset_shape`, pre-submit at zero quota. It collapsed TWO structures, both last-write-wins and both silent: `verdict_maps[document_id]` (the artifact could contradict the exit code) and `select_items`' `by_id` (a `--document` selector silently resolved to whichever item came last). Refused rather than de-duplicated — two items sharing a document_id carry different goldens, so there is no correct pick. The row's **final clause** ("must not let the artifact disagree with the gate") is now pinned *directly* by a count-equality assertion (Zangado F-7), not just implied by key uniqueness |
| **DEBT-52** | 🔶 **NARROWED, not closed — and the reason recorded was wrong.** `O_NOFOLLOW` binds the containment check to the descriptor actually read, closing the final-component swap. The residual is an intermediate-DIRECTORY swap. The code claimed that needed `openat` and was "not portable to the macOS target"; **that is false** (Zangado F-5) — the row's option B, `os.stat` at check time vs `os.fstat` after open comparing `(st_dev, st_ino)`, is POSIX and catches it, because inode identity does not care how the path was walked. Corrected in-code. **Remaining work:** option B needs the identity to cross the `IDPAdapter.extract` Protocol seam, which is an architectural change, not a local hardening |
| **DEBT-54 A-2** | ✅ **CLOSED.** `MAX_DOCUMENT_BYTES` (100 MiB) via `os.fstat` on the open descriptor. `del file_bytes` after the body append makes the documented ~2x peak true — review measured THREE live copies at `Request(...)` |
| **DEBT-57 A-4/A-5** | ✅ **CLOSED — found already implemented AND pinned**, not re-fixed. The row said both halves were open; `facade.py` applies `.strip()` to `item_id`, and `_log_run_end` is wrapped in `contextlib.suppress` |
| **DEBT-59** | ✅ **CLOSED.** Pin only, no production change; parametrized over `KeyboardInterrupt` and `SystemExit` |
| **DEBT-73 residual** | ✅ **CLOSED.** Five N818 renames, per-file-ignore removed |
| **DEBT-84** | ✅ **CLOSED — and the row understated the defect.** It described the race as "two `start()` calls within microseconds"; it is `is_busy()` (which the UI **polls**) versus `start()`. Measured **64 spurious 409s per 5000 acquires (~1.3%)**. The row's own prescribed fix — a registry-local flag consulted before probing — does **not** close it: when a job runs locally `start()` would correctly fail anyway, and when none runs (the spurious case) `is_busy()` still probes. Fixed instead with an opt-in bounded retry in `acquire()` that only `start()` uses: a probe holds the lock for microseconds, a real job for minutes, so a 0.25s budget separates them without masking a genuine conflict. Measured **0 per 5000**, genuine conflict still refused in ~0.26s |

### Two NEW defects found by QA while investigating a once-per-hundreds-of-runs flake

The flake was real, and it was the observable edge of two production defects — not a test problem.
Filed and fixed here as **DEBT-85**.

- **DEBT-85(a) — ✅ CLOSED.** `_persist` truncated the real file in place (`O_TRUNC` + buffered
  write), so the record was EMPTY on disk between truncate and flush. `load_history` skips an
  unparseable record *silently*, so a reader in that window — or a console that died in it — lost
  a paid-for job's record with no error. Now a temp file in the same directory plus `os.replace`.
  Same "the evidence disappears" class as DEBT-83, one layer out.
- **DEBT-85(b) — ✅ CLOSED.** `running` was never persisted: `start()` wrote the record while
  `Job.status` still held its default `planning`, and the transition touched only memory. Since
  `load_history` remaps only `running`, **a job killed mid-batch came back as `planning` forever**
  — non-terminal, with no note that real extractions had been spent. The INTERRUPTED path could
  therefore never fire in production. Its existing test hand-wrote a `status: "running"` record
  production never produced, so it constrained its own setup and passed throughout; it is
  rescoped to what it actually pins, and the end-to-end path is now driven through a real
  `start()` plus a simulated console death.

### QA round 2 (Zangado): N-1 … N-4, and a self-referential assertion

- **N-1 — ✅ fixed.** `test_a_genuinely_held_workspace_is_still_refused` called the DEFAULT
  `acquire()`, so the retry loop broke on its first attempt and the test's timing bound
  constrained nothing; a mutant inflating the budget ×100 (a retry that waits out a real job)
  passed the whole file. **The first fix was also wrong**, and instructively so: bounding
  `elapsed` against `START_LOCK_RETRY_SECONDS` is self-referential, because that is the constant
  the mutant changes — the bound moved with the thing it was bounding and the test passed while
  taking 25 seconds. Now an ABSOLUTE bound (a 409 answers an HTTP request, so it must be prompt
  whatever the budget says) plus a separate assertion that the constant itself stays ≤ 1s.
- **N-2 — ✅ fixed, both halves.** (a) `load_history` now treats an on-disk `planning` exactly as
  `running`: once the owning process is gone the two are equally dead, which makes the
  publish-then-persist ordering irrelevant to what an operator is told. (b) the two
  `start()`-driven tests poll the DISK record rather than memory, so they no longer inherit the
  window they were written to catch. A mutant removing `planning` from the dead set survived the
  entire UI suite until a direct unit pin was added for it.
- **N-3 — ✅ fixed.** `_persist`'s temp file is per-writer (`tempfile.mkstemp`) rather than keyed
  on the pid alone.
- **N-4 — ✅ fixed.** The prober loop yields, so a future cheaper `is_busy()` cannot silently turn
  that test into a slow one.
- **DEBT-52 row** — Zangado's added qualification recorded: the residual requires write access to
  an intermediate directory *on the root's path*, i.e. an attacker who already owns the corpus
  location, so the `Low` severity stands on its own reasoning rather than on the deferral.

### `/test` stamp — PASS, and what it found that two reviews had not

`docs/qa/TEST-LANE-D-F-2026-09-25.md`. Independence: implementer Opus 5 · APPROVE by
Atchim/Sonnet · gate by a **fresh Atchim instance on Fable 5.1** that did not issue the APPROVE
(**DEBT-44** honoured). It wrote its own mutants rather than reusing mine: **27 killed, 8
survived**.

Three findings, all non-blocking, **all since closed and mutation-verified**:

- **F-3 (Med) — a coverage REGRESSION introduced by my own N-1 fix.** At `5ab2f41` the
  default-budget path was exercised incidentally by the genuinely-held test; N-1 correctly moved
  that test onto the budget path and nothing replaced what it had been covering. A 30-second
  DEFAULT budget, and `is_busy()` opting into the budget, both survived — the only symptom is
  slowness, and no test asserted on speed. Now pinned structurally via `inspect.signature` and a
  recording `acquire`, not by timing.
- **F-2 (Low-Med) — the torn-write test was narrower than its own docstring.** A mutant that
  serializes first then truncates-in-place survived 3/3: the window shrinks to one syscall, so the
  slowed `json.dumps` no longer widens anything, yet `load_history` would still silently skip a
  reader landing in it. Write-then-rename **is** the invariant and is now asserted directly.
- **F-1 (Low) — a test name claiming an ordering it cannot prove.** Renamed to what it proves.

**Register correction it produced:** DEBT-59's Wave-0 "genuinely unpinned" text was **already
false at `358f16b~1`** — `3bff5f0` had pinned `KeyboardInterrupt` at the tail marker. Lane D's
real contribution there is the `SystemExit` parameter. Four mutants are accepted as unobservable
from a unit test (`del file_bytes`, `os.fsync`, the temp-cleanup `raise`, the per-writer
`mkstemp`) and recorded so they are not re-discovered as gaps.

### Process note, recorded because it is the point

Three of the defects above were found by review, not by the suite: the vacuous size-cap pin
(found independently by **both** reviewers), the false portability claim, and DEBT-85. One of my
own replacement tests then survived its mutant on the first attempt — a hammer-the-file
atomicity test that passed against the broken implementation because the window is microseconds
— and was only caught by running the mutation. It now widens the window deterministically. That
is the eighth vacuous test this project has found, and the first one caught by its author before
review.

**And then it happened twice more in the fixes themselves** — a mutant removing the `planning`
remap survived the whole UI suite, and N-1's *first* correction replaced one vacuous assertion
with another by bounding a value against the constant the mutation changes. Both were caught only
by re-running the mutants after fixing, never by the tests passing. The rule this project keeps
re-learning, stated once more: **a green test is evidence of nothing until the mutation it claims
to kill has been run against it** — and that includes the mutation run against the fix for a
vacuous test.

**A fourth was then found by the `/test` gate, and it was of a different kind:** not a test that
passed for the wrong reason, but a test that stopped covering something when a *neighbouring* test
was correctly fixed (F-3). Fixing N-1 moved the only test exercising the default-budget path onto
the budget path. Nothing went red, no assertion became false, and two mutants quietly started
surviving. The lesson generalises past "mutate after fixing": **when a fix moves what a test
exercises, check what that test used to cover incidentally** — coverage can be lost by a correct
change to a correct test.

### DEBT-22 leg 2 / ASM-01 — CLOSED 2026-09-25 by a live probe

✅ **The terminal status is `FAILED`.** Probed live: a truncated PDF (header intact, xref/trailer
chopped) was accepted by submit and reached `status: "FAILED"` in **3.2 s**. Two extractions from
the authorised budget — the first proved a non-success terminal status was reached, the second
read the string, since `capture_raw.py` deliberately swallows it (INV-02).

**The captured body, now `tests/fixtures/live/failed-execution.raw.json`:**
`{"documentName": …, "fields": {}, "id": …, "status": "FAILED", "tables": {}}`

**Read that shape carefully — it is why the default mattered.** There is no error object, no
message and no reason code. `fields` and `tables` are present and **empty**, so the only thing
distinguishing a hard IDP failure from a document where the extractor legitimately found nothing
is `status`. With `FAILED` absent from the default terminal set, the poll loop treated it as
non-terminal, waited out the **entire** execution budget (120 s as configured) and aborted as
`IDPPollTimeoutError` — `hard_failure` reported to the operator as "IDP was slow", about a
document IDP had already rejected in three seconds.

**Fixed:** the default is now `SUCCEEDED,FAILED` — terminal but not success, per ADR-0004 #5.
`PARTIAL_SUCCESS` deliberately stays out until ASM-01 confirms this org emits it, so an unknown
status is still non-terminal and still fails closed.

**Why no test caught it, which is the part worth keeping.** Two things pointed the wrong way at
once: the shared test helper (`tests/adapter/test_idp_client.py:152`) defaulted
`terminal_statuses` to `{"SUCCEEDED", "FAILED"}` — **broader than production shipped** — so every
poll test ran against a config the product did not have; and
`test_default_terminal_statuses_do_not_include_failed` **asserted the defect was correct**, with
a comment describing the mechanism (*"FAILED is not in the default set, so the loop must keep
waiting"*) and never arguing for it. A test that constrains the setup rather than an invariant,
cementing the behaviour it should have questioned — the same shape as the `/api/health` route
count, where a green test asserted a false list. It is replaced by two tests: `FAILED` raises
`IDPExecutionFailedError` with `status == "FAILED"`, and a genuinely unknown status
(`PARTIAL_SUCCESS`) still polls to the deadline, so broadening the default did not loosen the
fail-closed rule.

**ASM-01 moves from "open" to "partially confirmed":** `SUCCEEDED` and `FAILED` are both
confirmed live. Whether this org emits `PARTIAL_SUCCESS` is still unconfirmed and is still the
open half.

**2. DEBT-74 is bigger than filed, and it is now a contract that contradicts a live capture.**
`docs/design/CONTRACTS.md:7` still describes CT-01 as *"the internal shape `normalize()` emits
from a raw IDP `pages[]` body"* and still carries *"against a synthetic, scrubbed fixture (S-01.6
hasn't captured a real one yet)"*. Both halves are false: two live captures are committed, and
**`1d78c32` proved `pages[]` does not exist in any IDP response**. The contract of record
describes a wire shape the live API never emits — a strictly worse failure than a stale status
cell, and exactly the drift **DEBT-29**'s missing doc-lint would catch.

## ✅ WAVE-0 REGISTER AUDIT — 2026-09-23 (Dunga), every open row re-verified against HEAD `49939b2`

**Why it happened.** The register lagging reality is itself a filed defect (**DEBT-54**), and `REGRESSIONS.md` already carries a row that read "still OPEN" after the work had landed. Fixing an already-fixed row wastes a lane and produces a "fix" with no failing test behind it — which is how a suite acquires tests asserting that the code agrees with itself. So every open row was re-checked **by running and grepping at HEAD**, never by reading the row's own prose, before any Wave-1 lane starts.

**Result: the real backlog is 34 table rows, not 57.** Twenty were already closed by events and nobody had said so.

| 🔶 **still open** | **34** | 04, 05, 09, 11, 14, 17, 21, 22, 25, 27, 29, 31, 33, 37, 40, 41, 42, 43, 44, 46, 49, 52, 53, 54, 57, 59, 62, 63, 64, 65, 66, 67, 69, 71 |
| 🔁 **superseded** | **2** | 28 (→ ADR-0005 `:166` + Epic E F8), 50 (→ DEBT-63, the same defect filed later and more precisely) |
| ⛔ **WONTFIX** | **1** | 10 (accepted by design — the code matches the contract the row argues for) |

**Lane assignment for the 34 (this is the Wave-1 input).**
- **A — classifier:** 04, 05, 09, 11, 63
- **B — adapter:** 21 (leg 2 only), 22 (leg 2 only), 25, 49, 65, 67, 69, 71 · +NEW 78, 79
- **C — platform:** 14, 17, 27, 42, 53, 66 · + prose DEBT-53
- **D — orchestration:** 52, 54, 57, 59, 64
- **E — tooling/config:** 29, 31, 33, 37, 41 · +NEW 73, 75
- **WAVE 2 — process/review, serial:** 40, 43, 44, 46, 62 · + prose DEBT-46/47 · +NEW 72, 77
- **Docs / needs a decision first:** NEW 74, 76 · prose DEBT-48 (narrowed to FO-6) · prose DEBT-55 (**user decision**)

⚠️ **Four rows were SPLIT rather than closed — read these before starting a lane, they are the ones most likely to be mis-read as done:**
1. **DEBT-21** — the mid-poll 401 refresh landed; the submit-401 → `auth_failure` mapping (QA S-01.2 F-5) did **not**.
2. **DEBT-22** — CT-01 was re-pointed to the live capture; the terminal-status enum leg is untouched and still gated on S-01.6.
3. **DEBT-20 / DEBT-53 (table)** — the record-phase deadline **mechanism** landed; it ships **disabled with no env knob**, so the record phase has no bound in production. Do not read DEBT-20's closure as "there is a deadline".
4. **DEBT-33** — the wrong/dead `type: ignore` codes are gone (`mypy src tests` → 0 errors); the `[tool.mypy] packages` scope line is unchanged.

⚠️ **DEBT-46 was closed on 2026-09-22 and is FALSE AGAIN at HEAD.** S-01.1 and S-01.3 are fresh; **S-01.2 re-staled** (`367065b..HEAD` on `adapter/` = 5 files, +587/−37). A closure is a point-in-time fact about a moving tree, which is exactly why this audit re-ran the check instead of trusting the note.

⚠️ **Seven rows carried their closure evidence in the **Origin** cell while the Status cell still read `open`** — 45, 46, 55, 56, 58, 60, 61. Any tool (or human) reading the Status column saw seven closed items as open. That is a *structural* register defect, not seven clerical ones, and it belongs to **DEBT-29**'s doc-lint widened to compare register cells against each other — the generalisation DEBT-54's own closure flagged as unowned.

**Eight new rows were filed by this audit, none fixed: DEBT-72 … DEBT-79.** The two that change other rows' prices: **DEBT-73** (the dead-`noqa` class is 71 sites across five unselected rule families, ~10× DEBT-37/41's estimate) and **DEBT-72** (S-01.4's `/test` stamp is stale and no row covered it).

**Scope note:** this audit changed **registers and state only**. No `src/`, `tests/`, `docs/adr/`, `docs/specs/` or `docs/design/` file was touched; needed changes there are recorded as rows with named owners. Gates green at the audit commit: `pytest -q` → **1037 passed, 15 skipped**; `mypy src tests` → **0 errors, 93 files**; `ruff check src tests` → clean; `gitleaks git .` → no leaks, 267 commits.

| ~~DEBT-17~~ | `record_run` (ADR-0005 #9) relies on langfuse==4.15.4 internals. `run_experiment` reads items by duck typing (`id`/`dataset_id`/`input`/`expected_output`/`metadata`) and exposes `ExperimentItemResult.trace_id`/`dataset_run_id`. It also swallows failures, so the only failure signals are ERROR logs on the `langfuse` and OTLP-exporter loggers. A SDK bump can silently change any of these. The `record_experiment` log watcher (`tracing.py`) is process-wide by design (R5) — in a process that runs multiple `LangfuseAdapter`/`Langfuse` clients concurrently (e.g. a test suite, never the production CLI which is one client per process), the watcher installed for ONE `record_run` call can also observe an ERROR logged by a DIFFERENT client's concurrent OTLP export or dataset-run-item call. Confirmed 2026-09-19 while fixing a test-order-dependent failure in `test_inv01_payload.py` (fixed there via subprocess isolation, not by narrowing the watcher). Harmless for the real CLI (one client per process); a future multi-client-in-one-process use (e.g. a long-running service) would need per-call watcher scoping (e.g. thread-local or a correlation id filter) to stay correct. | src/idp_regression/platform/langfuse_adapter.py (`record_run`), tracing.py | coupling | Medium: a silent change could drop Experiments-tab linkage or hide a record failure. (1) Per-call scoping: `tracing._RECORD_EXPERIMENT_LOCK` makes `record_experiment` calls in one process mutually exclusive, so no two watcher windows overlap. It landed before this audit and the row was never updated. (2) Re-verifying the SDK contract on every bump is now a test, `tests/platform/test_sdk_contract.py`, run against the REAL installed langfuse. It pins the `run_experiment` keywords `record_experiment` passes, the result attributes it reads (`item_results`, `trace_id`, `dataset_run_id`, `item.id`), and the `hasattr(item, "id")`/`hasattr(item, "dataset_id")` duck typing that links items to a dataset run. | DEBT-27 | **Log-watch failure detection is coupled to undocumented SDK logging behaviour (sibling of DEBT-17).** ADR-0005 #9's only failure signal for the record phase is `_FailureWatcher` watching two named loggers for ERROR records: `opentelemetry.exporter.otlp.proto.http.trace_exporter` and `langfuse` (`tracing.py`, watcher sets `self.failed = True`; `FlushFailedError`/`ExperimentRecordFailedError` raised after the `finally`). Two residuals: (a) **FALSE NEGATIVE** — a `BatchSpanProcessor` queue drop logs on `opentelemetry.sdk.trace.export`, typically at WARNING, which neither watcher sees; and a patch release that downgrades any watched ERROR to WARNING silently disables detection **with no red test**, because the unit tests synthesise the ERROR record on those loggers themselves instead of provoking a real export failure. (b) **FALSE POSITIVE** — logging handlers are process-wide, so ANY ERROR on the `langfuse` logger inside the record window aborts the run, including one unrelated to this experiment, producing a false FAIL on another team's prompt-change PR. Candidate fixes: widen the watched logger set to the `opentelemetry.sdk.trace.export` parent and drop the level floor to WARNING for drop-class records; add a live probe test that provokes a real OTLP export failure (see REG-08); scope the watcher per call (correlation id / thread-local) to kill the false positive. | src/idp_regression/platform/tracing.py (`_FailureWatcher`, `record_experiment`); src/idp_regression/platform/langfuse_adapter.py (`record_run`); docs/adr/0005-evaluation-platform-redecision.md (#9) | coupling | **Med-High (impact re-framed 2026-09-20 to match REG-08's corrected class — the original "reports a broken run GREEN" wording is superseded).** False negative: a run **exits 0 while its evidence never landed** — the *verdict* is still sound (INV-08, `docs/design/INVARIANTS.md:14`: the gate is computed before the platform write and never inferred from what landed), but the *record* is silently incomplete, breaking INV-04 reproducibility and any Epic E baseline read, and it is found long after the fact. False positive: a healthy run aborts on an unrelated process-wide `langfuse` ERROR — a false FAIL on another team's prompt-change PR. **ADR-0005 #9 amendment A1 (Soneca) rules that neither residual dominates — false positive is worse per incident, false negative worse over time — so a fix MUST NOT silently trade one away for the other.** Neither is caught by the current suite | 3 | **Grows on every `langfuse` / `opentelemetry` version bump** — the coupling is to logger *names* and *levels* that no SDK treats as public API, and the synthetic unit tests stay green when detection goes dark, so the interest is paid silently | 🔶 **NARROWED 2026-09-27 (Wave B, `b3e7e43`) — and leg (a) was found STILL OPEN after being called fixed.** (a) FALSE NEGATIVE: the earlier fix watched `opentelemetry.sdk.trace.export`, but opentelemetry-sdk 1.44.0 logs "Queue full, dropping %s." and "Exception while exporting %s." on `opentelemetry.sdk._shared_internal`, which is not a child of that logger. A dropped span or a failed batch export still read GREEN, and the tests passed because they synthesised the record on the watched logger. **Now closed properly:** the watcher is on the parent `opentelemetry.sdk`, and tests drive the REAL `BatchSpanProcessor` into a drop and a raising export (the original logger name fails them). (b) FALSE POSITIVE across concurrent calls: closed by `_RECORD_EXPERIMENT_LOCK`. **Still open, Low:** an unrelated `langfuse` ERROR logged INSIDE one call's own window still aborts that run. | DEBT-43 | **The value-type obligations of a guard are still NOT derived from the declared type — only the presence obligations are. Explicit sibling of DEBT-40 and of the PROCESS AMENDMENT below: the instrument's scope was narrower than the class it was meant to cover.** FU-01.3-D part (3) made the **key-presence** pins type-driven via `sorted(DocumentRecord.__required_keys__)`, and the stamp then claimed "a fourth escape is structurally unavailable". It was not: `__required_keys__` says **which keys must exist**, never **what type each value must be** — so the value checks stayed hand-enumerated (`item_id` yes at `langfuse_adapter.py:112`, `document_id` no) and **the fourth escape landed on the un-instrumented axis within one day**, fail-open (QA-01 re-audit #3 F-1 → FU-01.3-G). **The rule: for every field the guard protects, derive BOTH obligations mechanically — presence from `__required_keys__`, value type from `typing.get_type_hints(T)` — and parametrize both. A type-driven presence pin standing next to a hand-written value pin is not a type-driven guard; it is a hand-written guard with one automated column.** 🔗 **Read with DEBT-40** (mutant population scoped to changed lines → a defect *above* the guard invisible) **and the PROCESS AMENDMENT** (debt card scoped to a line number → a twin *beside* the original invisible). **All three are one failure at three scopes**, and this row is the sharpest of them because the narrowing happened *inside a fix explicitly designed to stop hand-enumeration*. | src/idp_regression/platform/langfuse_adapter.py:101-114 (`_require_record_shape`) and :278 (`get_dataset`); src/idp_regression/platform/types.py:49-66 (`DocumentRecord`); tests/platform/test_record_run_preconditions.py (the `__required_keys__` parametrize, presence-only) | process/test | **Med — and, unlike its two siblings, this one has already produced a FAIL-OPEN production defect, not just an escaped review.** Every future guard in this repo inherits the same half-instrumented shape: a reviewer sees "parametrized over the declared type" and reasonably concludes the type is fully enforced | 1 (the mechanical part rides FU-01.3-G (iii); this row is the *rule*) | **Compounds with every scalar field added to a `TypedDict`**: each new field silently auto-generates a presence pin and silently generates **no** value pin, so the gap widens by one on each schema change while the tests keep *looking* type-driven | 🔶 **still open — Wave-0 audit 2026-09-23 (Dunga), re-verified at HEAD `49939b2`:** **WAVE 2 (process, serial).** The rule text is now correct in this register (DEBT-54 amended it), but the rule is still owed to the shared protocol, which has not been amended (grepped at HEAD — see DEBT-40). | ~~DEBT-45~~ | **T-01.4.10 leg 1 must be REDEFINED before it is wired into CI — and the work had NO REAL ID until today.** FU-01.3-D's DoD (h) deferred it to "already **DEBT-1** on the 2026-09-21 list"; **`DEBT-01` is the unrelated ADR-0004 best-effort-marker row, and Zangado could not find the referenced card anywhere.** The pointer pointed at nothing, so the work had no owner and no price. **This row IS that id. Owner: Dengoso. Executed on `T-01.4.10` in S-01.4, where it is priced (`RE-ESTIMATE PENDING`).** The defect: leg 1 is a **blocking `gitleaks git .` over full history on every PR** — its verdict is a function of the past rather than of the change under review, any historical hit is permanently un-closeable except by rewriting published history (*"a control whose only remedy is a destructive operation is not a control"*), it is O(history) per PR, and it conflates *"does this PR introduce a secret"* (blocking, diff-scoped) with *"does history contain one"* (periodic audit, whose correct answer is rotation). **Redefinition:** merge-base-scoped blocking gate + `gitleaks protect --staged` pre-commit + a separate **non-blocking scheduled** full-history audit with a triaged baseline. ⚠️ **AND — verified by Zangado, do not re-derive — merge-base scoping ALONE does not clear this branch:** `gitleaks git . --log-opts="$(git merge-base main HEAD)..HEAD"` still returns **1**, because merge-base is `d3d1444`, **129 commits back**, putting `353549d` *inside this PR's own range*. **So the `.gitleaks.toml` `[allowlist] fingerprints` entry (one commit × file × line × rule, same-line reason) is the LOAD-BEARING remedy, not an optional extra.** ⛔ Never a `paths = ['tests/']` allowlist — fixtures are the highest-risk file class in the repo. No history rewrite; squash-merge is a merge mode, not a control. | `.gitleaks.toml` (to be created — `[allowlist] fingerprints`); the CI workflow + pre-commit config landed by `T-01.4.10`; `docs/specs/SPEC-01-baseline-regression.md` T-01.4.10 + S-01.4 DoD line | tooling/security | **Med.** Today: no credential is exposed — the `353549d` literal is **verified not a credential** (matches no live `.env` value; `.env` gitignored + untracked), **no rotation**. But leg 1 as currently written will go red on the first PR it gates, and a gate that is red for a non-reason gets disabled — which is how the repo ends up with no secret gate at all | inherits T-01.4.10's `RE-ESTIMATE PENDING` (Dengoso) | **Grows with history length, by construction** — every commit makes the full-history scan slower and adds another permanently un-closeable finding surface. And while `Secrets: ⚠️` stands as a standing caveat, a real future hit is harder to distinguish from the known one | ✅ **already-closed — Wave-0 audit 2026-09-23 (Dunga), verified at HEAD `49939b2`:** **The closure claim in the Origin cell is TRUE at HEAD; only the column was wrong.** All three legs exist and are real: `.github/workflows/secrets-gate.yml` job `gitleaks-diff` computes `BASE="$(git merge-base origin/$BASE_REF HEAD)"` and scans `--log-opts="${BASE}..HEAD"` (genuinely merge-base scoped, not full history); `.githooks/pre-commit` runs `gitleaks git --staged .`; the `env-hygiene` job asserts `.env` ignored and untracked; full history moved to the **separate scheduled** `secrets-audit.yml` with `continue-on-error: true`. `.gitleaksignore` holds exactly one fingerprint (`353549d…:tests/platform/test_langfuse_adapter.py:generic-api-key:305`) — not a `paths` blind. Live at HEAD: `gitleaks git . --redact` → **no leaks found**, exit 0, 267 commits. The human decision was taken and the redefinition shipped. · **prior Status cell, preserved verbatim:** open — **named owner: Dengoso**, executed on T-01.4.10 | closed 2026-09-22 (QA S-01.4 F-2) — all three legs + the scheduled audit shipped, and the load-bearing `.gitleaksignore` fingerprint (not a paths blind) is in place; scan green. | ~~DEBT-80~~ | **A date-format prompt regression produces a GREEN build — found live, 2026-09-24, TEST-PLAN Phase 7.** The degraded `1.1.0` prompt (ISO-8601 instruction dropped from `invoice_date`) changed exactly one cell across 45: `inv-002-format-variance.pdf` `invoice_date` `2026-01-22` → `22/01/2026`. `_format_date` parses both to the same calendar date, so the verdict is `wrong_format`, and BR3/`overall_gate` fail only on `missing`/`wrong_value` — `exit_code=0`, `pass_count=5`. **No code is misbehaving:** the classifier does exactly what ADR-0003 specifies. The defect is that **TEST-PLAN 7.3 and `idp-action-definition.md:61` both assert this degradation "should flip `overall_gate` from PASS to FAIL"**, which BR3 forbids — TEST-PLAN 7.3 even writes "`gate=FAIL`, with the degraded field showing `wrong_value` or `wrong_format`", an outcome its own design rules cannot produce. One of the two must change: either BR3's treatment of `wrong_format` on a `critical` field, or both documents' claim. **Needs a product decision (Feliz/Soneca), not a code fix.** ⚠️ Weigh it as what it is: the product's stated purpose is catching prompt regressions, and `idp-action-definition.md` names this one as *"the single most common real-world prompt regression for date fields"*. Today the gate is green for it. | `src/idp_regression/classifier/gate.py` (`overall_gate`, BR3); `docs/adr/0003-classifier-and-gate.md` (BR3/AC4); `testpack/TEST-PLAN.md` §7.3; `testpack/idp-action-definition.md:41-61` | contract/product | **High — a real prompt regression the tool exists to catch returns exit 0.** Fail-OPEN, the worst failure class for a CI gate (see CLAUDE.md ## Rigor) | 2-3 | Grows the moment another team trusts this gate on prompt changes | ✅ **CLOSED 2026-09-24 — decided by the user, implemented, pinned, mutation-verified.** **Decision: a per-field opt-in (`format_critical`), not a change to BR3's default.** `GoldenField.format_critical: bool` (optional, default `false`); when true, `overall_gate` fails a `wrong_format` verdict on that field as it does a `wrong_value`. `Verdict` carries the flag because `overall_gate` sees only the verdict map, never the golden, and reads it with `.get(..., False)` so a map built before this key existed keeps the old behaviour instead of raising `KeyError`. **BR3's default is deliberately untouched** — a semantically-correct value formatted differently is still not a regression; this is the declared exception for a field whose format IS the contract. Independent of `critical`: neither implies the other. **Scope is fields only**; `tables`/`prompts` have no equivalent (row/column comparison is `text`, whose format tier only normalises whitespace, so the same fail-open is far narrower there) — extending it is a separate decision, not taken. **Rejected:** (a) make `wrong_format` fail whenever `critical` — one line, no schema change, but turns every cosmetic difference red and discards BR3 wholesale; (b) leave BR3 and just fix the test plan — cheapest and defensible, rejected because this tool's whole value is gating prompt changes and a prompt that stops honouring its own stated output format is exactly what a reviewer must see. **Pins** (`tests/classifier/test_gate.py`): `test_wrong_format_fails_the_gate_when_the_field_is_format_critical` (the live 1.1.0 regression as a unit test), `test_wrong_format_still_passes_when_format_critical_is_absent` (BR3's default, guards against a global application), `test_format_critical_does_not_fail_the_gate_on_a_match`, `test_format_critical_is_independent_of_critical`, `test_overall_gate_tolerates_a_verdict_map_without_format_critical`, `test_non_bool_format_critical_is_a_malformed_golden`. **Mutation-verified:** branch-removed and flag-always-true mutants each RED (2 failures apiece), source restored byte-identical by `shasum`. **Contract written down:** ADR-0003 Amendment 2026-09-24; DATA-MODEL-01 §1 + §3; CT-02 widened; committed golden schema gains the property (1,935 minified chars vs CT-05's 10,000 cap, Ajv strict clean — no new `if`/`then`, no new `required`). **Corrected the two documents that made the false claim:** TEST-PLAN §7.3 (which had asserted `gate=FAIL` *and* `wrong_format`, an outcome its own rules forbid) and `idp-action-definition.md`. `invoice_date` is the only field in the testpack that opts in. ⚠️ **Operational step, not optional:** the committed schema's hash changed (`13f9b7bf…` live vs `88cdbf0d…` committed, measured), so the Langfuse dataset MUST be re-provisioned or every run aborts `schema_drift`. · **prior Status cell, preserved verbatim:** 🔶 **OPEN — decision required before this tool gates another team's prompt changes.** Live evidence: run `auto-detected-1.1.0` (experiment `auto-detected-1.1.0-0dcb93d8`), diffed against baseline `baseline-1.0.0-r2`. | TEST-PLAN Phase 7 live run, 2026-09-24 |

Verified: expected `2026-02-01` vs actual `02/01/2026` → `wrong_format` (**false negative** — in a DD/MM document that is 2 Jan, a genuinely different date); expected `2026-01-02` vs actual `02/01/2026` → `wrong_value` (**false positive** — it *is* 2 Jan). Found while diagnosing DEBT-80; `inv-002` escaped it only because `22` cannot be a month. Options: drop the ambiguous formats and require the golden's own type to declare a locale; or make an ambiguous parse a distinct non-green verdict rather than guessing. | `src/idp_regression/classifier/canonical.py:31-42` (`_DATE_FORMATS`, `_format_date`) | correctness | Med — both a false GREEN and a false RED are reachable on ordinary European-format invoices; no current fixture has a day ≤ 12 in a slash-formatted date, so the suite cannot see it | 2 | Grows with any non-US golden; a single day-≤-12 invoice exposes it | ✅ **CLOSED — Wave 2 D2b, `8f4de93`** (declared `date_format`; the undeclared M/D/Y default pinned). | ~~DEBT-82~~ | **`provision_golden_dataset.py` appended instead of upserting, silently duplicating every golden item.** The item POST carried no `id`, so Langfuse minted a fresh one per invocation. Observed live 2026-09-24: re-provisioning after the `format_critical` change left `idp-generator-test1` with **10 items**, 5 refreshed and 5 stale, and the next run processed every document **twice** (double IDP quota, `items=10`). Worse, the stale copy of `inv-002-format-variance.pdf` **PASSED the very regression its refreshed twin caught**, because that golden predates the `format_critical` opt-in — the dataset was simultaneously enforcing two different contracts. | `scripts/provision_golden_dataset.py` (`_build_item_payload`) | impl/tooling | **High — it silently doubles IDP quota per run and lets a stale golden mask a real regression**; it fires on exactly the workflow the docs prescribe | 1 | Compounds on every re-provision (10 → 15 → 20 items) | ✅ **CODE FIXED 2026-09-24** — the payload now carries `id = uuid5(_ITEM_NAMESPACE, "<dataset>|<document_id>")`, a pinned namespace distinct from `platform/scoring.py`'s, so re-provisioning is an idempotent upsert (same N26 pattern as the score writes). ⚠️ **`idp-generator-test1` is abandoned, not repaired** — it still carries 10 items (5 stale). Item deletion was not available to the fixing session, so the clean path taken was a fresh dataset, **`idp-generator-test2`**, provisioned under the fixed script: 5 items, all carrying `format_critical`, and **idempotency confirmed live** (a repeat provision returned the same `item_id` and left the count at 5). `idp-generator-test1` should be deleted or ignored; do not run against it. **No test yet:** the script is untested by the suite (it is `scripts/`, not `src/`), which is itself why this shipped — worth a test that asserts two consecutive `_build_item_payload` calls yield the same `id`. | Found by running TEST-PLAN Phase 7.3, 2026-09-24 |
| ~~DEBT-83~~ | **The local run artifact collapses duplicate `document_id`s last-write-wins, losing the evidence for the failure that set the exit code.** `facade.py:714` does `verdict_maps[document_id] = verdicts`, so when one run processes the same `document_id` more than once, only the last verdict map survives into `.idp-regression-run-artifacts/<run_id>.json`. Observed live 2026-09-24 (run `regressed-1.1.0`): the run processed **10** documents and returned `exit_code=1`, but the artifact holds **5**, and the surviving `inv-002-format-variance.pdf` record is the copy that **PASSED** (`format_critical: false`, `wrong_format`) — the FAILing copy that actually produced the non-zero exit was overwritten. An operator opening the artifact to explain a red build finds a verdict that cannot fail the gate. ADR-0007 designates this file as *the* place the values live (the platform is value-free by DEBT-18 option B), so this is the only record of what went wrong, and it is wrong. Same failure class as DEBT-09: a last-write-wins collapse that discards evidence rather than surfacing it. | `src/idp_regression/orchestration/facade.py:714`; `src/idp_regression/orchestration/run_artifact.py` (the `{run_id: {document_id: ...}}` address, ADR-0007) | correctness/observability | **Med-High — the artifact can contradict the exit code it is meant to explain.** Needs duplicate `document_id`s in one run, which DEBT-82 made easy and which a golden set may also produce legitimately | 2 | Any run over a dataset with a repeated `document_id` | ✅ **CLOSED — Lane D, `358f16b`** (evidence: § LANES D & F). ## Rulings on the 2026-09-21 re-audit rows (Dunga)

**DEBT-40 — where the review-method rule should live. Recommendation: the `code-review-protocol` skill, NOT only this register.** *(See also the PROCESS AMENDMENT at the end of this section — DEBT-40 and the grep-pattern carding rule are the same failure at two different scopes, and they should be read together.)*
A DEBT row records that a rule *should* exist; only the protocol makes the next reviewer follow it. This rule has already failed twice **by omission**, which is exactly the failure a register cannot prevent — nobody greps DEBT.md before writing a mutation matrix. Concretely, the ask is one clause in the plugin's TDD-gate / mutation-matrix section: *"when the change under review is a validation guard, derive the mutant population from the guarded type's declared fields (`TypedDict.__required_keys__`, dataclass fields, schema keys) and verify that no path subscripts any of them **before** the guard — a population drawn from the diff's changed lines cannot see an unguarded caller above the guard."* Same constraint as DEBT-31: the skill is **shared plugin tooling outside this repo**, so this project cannot land it unilaterally. Therefore: **keep DEBT-40 open as the in-repo record and the tracking handle until the protocol change lands**, and in the meantime state the obligation explicitly in each `/test` stamp's mutation-matrix section so it binds the next audit even without the plugin change. Recording it *only* here would be recording the lesson in the one place the person who needs it will not look.

**DEBT-41 vs DEBT-37 — keep two rows, run one card, and respect the ordering.**
They are **different questions**: DEBT-37 asks *"does a dead suppression get detected?"* (enable `RUF100`); DEBT-41 asks *"has the URL-scheme security family ever run?"* (enable `S3xx`). Merging them would let the trivial one close the pair and bury the security decision inside a hygiene card — the same burial that produced DEBT-41 in the first place. **They are cheaper together**: both edit the single `select` list in `pyproject.toml`, both concern `transport.py`, and both would otherwise each pay a `/test` re-stamp. So: **both ride FU-01.3-F**, two rows, one card, no estimate change beyond DEBT-41's own decision cost.
**⚠️ Ordering trap, and the reason this pairing is more than convenience.** FU-01.3-F's DoD deletes the `# noqa: S310` *as dead*. It is dead **only while `S` is unselected**. If DEBT-41 is later decided as *enable `S3xx`*, that rule may fire on the `urllib.request.urlopen` call inside the `_urlopen` seam and the deleted suppression would have to be re-derived — from scratch, by someone who no longer has this context. **So decide DEBT-41 FIRST, in the same card, before executing DEBT-37's deletion.** If `S3xx` is enabled, re-assess the line under the live rule and either fix it or write a *real* suppression with a reason comment; if `S3xx` is recorded out of scope, delete the `noqa` as DEBT-37 specifies. Either way the outcome is a comment that means what it says, which is the point both rows are making.

---

## PROCESS AMENDMENT (Dunga, 2026-09-21, from the FU-01.3-D `/test` re-stamp) — card dead-assertion debt by GREP PATTERN, not by LINE NUMBER

**The rule.** When a review finds a dead, vacuous or truthy-only assertion, the DEBT row and the card DoD must name the **mechanically greppable pattern**, and the fix must sweep it across `tests/`. A line number may appear only as *an example of* the pattern, never as the scope. The pattern that produced DEBT-35/DEBT-42 is: **`assert <subscript>` with no comparison operator** — i.e. `assert x["k"]` / `assert x[0]` standing alone, asserting only truthiness of a value the test already claims to know exactly. Concretely, a card carrying this class of debt closes only when a grep of the pattern over `tests/` comes back empty (or every remaining hit is individually justified in the card), **not** when the named line is gone.

**Why — the demonstrated cost, not a principle.** DEBT-35 was carded as `test_schema_provisioning.py:38`. FU-01.3-D deleted `:38` exactly as carded and closed the bullet **correctly**. The identical assertion at `:88`, three tests down in the same file, survived untouched — and was found only because an independent no-context coverage audit re-read the whole file. **A line-scoped card cannot see a copy three tests down.** The scope of the card, not the quality of the work, is what let the twin live: this is the second time in two days that a correctly-executed narrow artifact produced a wrong-looking outcome (cf. DEBT-40). One audit cycle, paid again.

**Corollary — the closure-wording rule.** When a card closes a line-scoped item whose class is *not* closed, the register row must say so in the status field itself (see DEBT-35, amended today to read closed **for `:38` specifically**, cross-referencing DEBT-42). Otherwise a future reader greps `DEBT-35`, sees `closed`, and concludes the pattern is gone from the file it is still in. **A closed bullet must never be readable as a closed class.**

> ✅ **The corollary caught ITSELF, on the day it was written — one artifact away, in the card that produced it.** QA-01 re-audit #3 finding **F-5**: `FU-01.3-D`'s header in `SPEC-01` read a bare `✅ DONE` while DoD (e) and DoD (h) were both open and *disclosed two bullets further down*. The disclosure was honest; **the header was not**, and a header is what a reader triages by. Reworded 2026-09-21 to `✅ DONE — (e) CLAUDE.md line and (h) exit condition carried, see below`. Recorded here rather than only on the card because the point is not the one header: **the rule was written against a DEBT-row status field and the same failure immediately appeared in a card title, which is evidence that the corollary's scope is "any closure marker a reader triages by" — register status, card header, DoD checkbox, stamp verdict — not just the register.** Worth exactly this much text, and no more: a rule that catches itself within hours is a rule that was describing a live pattern, not a principle.

**↔ DEBT-40 — YES, cross-reference them, in both directions. They are siblings, and the pair is the finding.**
DEBT-40: the **mutant population** was scoped to the diff's changed lines, so a defect *above* the guard was structurally invisible. This amendment: the **debt card** was scoped to one line number, so a copy *beside* the original was structurally invisible. Different artifacts, identical failure — *the scope of the instrument was narrower than the class it was meant to cover, and a correct result inside that scope read as a complete result*. Neither is a competence failure and neither is fixed by trying harder; both are fixed by deriving scope from something mechanical (the declared type's `__required_keys__` for DEBT-40; a grep pattern here) instead of from where the reviewer's eye happened to land. They also share a destination: the `lemon-studio-sdd:code-review-protocol` skill, **shared plugin tooling outside this repo**, so neither can land unilaterally here. **Therefore: keep both open as the in-repo tracking handles, state both obligations in each `/test` stamp's mutation-matrix section so they bind the next audit even without the plugin change, and land them as ONE protocol amendment when it goes in** — they argue the same point twice and are more convincing together than apart.

> ⚠️ **AMENDED 2026-09-21 (re-audit #3) — the pair is now a QUARTET, and the "one protocol amendment" ruling stands and gets stronger.** Two more rows joined the same destination on the same day:
> - **DEBT-43 — the type-driven instrument was itself scoped too narrowly.** `__required_keys__` instrumented **presence** and nothing instrumented **value type**, so the "fourth escape is structurally unavailable" claim failed within a day, fail-open. This is the **third scope** of the identical failure — DEBT-40: mutant population narrower than the class (a defect *above* the guard); the amendment: debt card narrower than the class (a twin *beside* the original); DEBT-43: the automated pin narrower than the class (an axis *orthogonal* to the original). **Cross-reference all three in both directions** — they are one finding stated three times, and DEBT-43 is the most persuasive of them because the narrowing occurred *inside the fix built to prevent narrowing*.
> - **DEBT-44 — reviewer-instance independence on the `/test` gate**, which is a *different* rule but the **same destination and the same landing vehicle**. It is now the **third argument** for spending the one protocol amendment: with 40 + the amendment + 43 + 44 in hand, the ask is no longer "add a clause", it is "the review protocol has one structural blind spot (scope derived from where the reviewer looked) and one structural conflict of interest (the gate run by the approver), and here are four dated, costed instances."
> **Ruling unchanged and now firmer: land DEBT-40, DEBT-43, DEBT-44 and this amendment as ONE amendment to `lemon-studio-sdd:code-review-protocol`. Do not split them, do not land any one of them alone**, and keep all four open in-repo as the tracking handles until it goes in.

**DEBT-42 — home ruling: FU-01.3-F, at NO estimate change. Dunga concurs with Atchim, and on substance rather than convenience.**
The convenience argument is real but secondary: all three edits are test-file-only, no production change, and they ride a `/test` re-stamp FU-01.3-F already pays — splitting them into a card of their own would buy a second re-stamp and nothing else (the same arithmetic that put DEBT-41 on that card). **The substantive argument is thematic coherence, and it is why F beats E.** FU-01.3-E also touches `tests/` (mypy scope) and was the near-miss home. But FU-01.3-F is already *the card about controls that look present and aren't*: DEBT-37 is a `noqa` that suppresses nothing, DEBT-41 is a rule family that was never enabled, and DEBT-42 part (1) is an assertion that can never fail. Same class, one reviewer's head, one sweep. Parts (2) and (3) — docstrings whose stated reason is not the real reason — are the documentation-side expression of exactly that class. **No ordering dependency** with DEBT-41's decision (that one is `pyproject.toml` + `transport.py`; this is `tests/platform/`), so the ordering trap recorded above is unaffected. **One condition on the card:** the prohibition on auto-synthesising `full_record`'s value must be copied onto the card DoD verbatim, not left in this register — per the routing rule (*"a baton sentence is not a backlog"*), an implementer reading only the card must see the thing they must not do.

> ⚠️ **Correction to the closure grep (orchestrator, 2026-09-21).** The pattern as first written — `rg 'assert \w+\[' tests/ | rg -v '=='` — **can never come back empty**, because it flags legitimate assertions that compare with something other than `==`. Verified false positives today: `tests/platform/test_golden_schema_contract.py:299` and `:300`, both `assert <subscript> is False`. A closure condition that cannot be satisfied is not a gate. Use a pattern that excludes every comparison/membership form, e.g. `rg 'assert [A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*\[[^]]*\]\s*(#.*)?$' tests/` — i.e. a subscript that is the **entire** assertion expression, with nothing after it but an optional comment. That matches `test_schema_provisioning.py:88` (the real target) and not the `is False` pair.

> 🔎 **The corrected grep immediately found a THIRD instance — which is the rule earning its keep on the day it was written.** Running the corrected pattern across `tests/` returns **two** live sites, not one: the known `tests/platform/test_schema_provisioning.py:88`, **and `tests/adapter/test_integration_idp.py:72` — `assert out["status"]`**, a truthy-only assertion in a *different directory* that no line-scoped or platform-scoped card would ever have surfaced. Context: it sits in the live-opt-in IDP test, one line above `assert isinstance(out["fields"], dict)`, and asserts only that `status` is non-empty. ⚠️ **CORRECTION (Zangado, QA-01 re-audit #3, 2026-09-21 — this row's original wording by the orchestrator was WRONG and is struck).** The original text claimed it "would pass for `FAILED`/`CANCELLED`" and was "arguably worse than the `:88` twin". **Both claims are false.** `IDPClient.extract()` returns `body` **only** when `raw_status in self._success_statuses` (`src/idp_regression/adapter/idp_client.py:314-317`); any other terminal status raises `IDPExecutionFailedError`, and `normalize()` raises again on any status outside the success set (`normalize.py:53`). So `out["status"]` is **success-only by construction** and the assertion cannot accept a failed status. It is **redundant, not dangerous** — a weaker finding than the `:88` twin, not a stronger one. Debt remains the right call; the urgency implied by the original wording was not. Correct substitution once S-01.6 pins the allowlist: `assert out["status"] in <success set>`. **Fold into DEBT-42** (same class, same one-line substitution shape — assert the specific expected status, per the S-01.6 terminal-status allowlist once that spike pins it). ⚠️ It is inside the `RUN_INTEGRATION_TESTS=1` live suite gated on a published IDP action id that does not exist yet (S-01.6), so it is **currently skipped and cannot be validated by running it** — fix it by reading, and re-check when S-01.6 unblocks the live IDP leg. This is exactly the "instrument scoped narrower than the class" failure that DEBT-40 and the process amendment describe: the class was never confined to `tests/platform/`, and nothing had ever looked outside it.

## DEBT-46 — INTAKE: a reviewer's "Debt to hand to Dunga" list has no mechanical intake step

**Status:** open (process) · **Origin:** QA-01 S-01.3 **re-audit #4**, 2026-09-21 · **Impact: High** — this one has now cost a rediscovery, and it is the *second* failure of the same rule.

> ⚖️ **WAVE-0 AUDIT 2026-09-23 (Dunga) — KEEP OPEN, and this audit is the FOURTH data point, not a counter-example.** The protocol amendment has not landed (grepped at HEAD — see the DEBT-47 block below), so the mechanical intake step is still owed. This audit again performed intake **by hand**, producing eight new rows (**DEBT-72 … DEBT-79**) from findings that had no route into the register otherwise — including two (**DEBT-72**, S-01.4's stale stamp; **DEBT-73**, the 71-site dead-`noqa` class) that materially change other rows' cost. Same verdict as 2026-09-22, one instance stronger: **all eight were intaken only because a human tasked an agent whose explicit job was intake.** That is scheduling, not a gate. Do not read "eight intaken" as evidence the process works.

> ⚖️ **DISPOSITION 2026-09-22 (Dunga triage) — KEEP OPEN. Owner: Dunga (in-repo tracking handle) → the shared `lemon-studio-sdd:code-review-protocol` amendment. Next action: land it as ONE amendment with DEBT-40 / DEBT-43 / DEBT-44 / DEBT-47 (the ruling at `## PROCESS AMENDMENT` stands); interim binding is the `(z) INTAKE` DoD bullet.**
> **And the answer to the question this triage was asked to answer is yes: doing it by hand again IS evidence the mechanical step is still owed.** An independent review of today's adapter deltas produced a `## Debt to hand to Dunga` list of **four** items. All four are now intaken — **DEBT-69** (systemic adapter fixture blindness), **DEBT-70** (retry-path URL unpinned, in flight), **DEBT-71** (`IDP_POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS` has no env var) and the fourth, the wire-contract regression PIN, recorded as **standing rule SR-1 in `REGRESSIONS.md`** and cross-referenced from DEBT-55. **Nothing evaporated this time — but only because a human tasked an agent with a triage whose explicit job was intake.** That is not a gate, it is a coincidence of scheduling: the mechanism that saved these four was a coordinator remembering, which is the same mechanism that lost re-audit #4's S-1 for four hours. **Third data point, same rule, unchanged verdict:** a convention binds the person who remembers it, which is never the person who forgets. Do not read "all four intaken" as evidence the process works.

**What happened.** Re-audit #4's finding F-1 (`get_dataset` type-checks `document_id` but not `item_id` — 1 of 2 `str` fields on `DatasetItem`) **had already been reported four hours earlier**, by Atchim instance #2, as suggestion **S-1** in its FU-01.3-G review, and repeated in its re-review under the heading `## Debt to hand to Dunga`. It was never carded. `DEBT.md` had no row for it (DEBT-43 records the general *rule*, not this gap). Surfaced → routed → evaporated → rediscovered by QA as a finding.

**Why this is worse than an ordinary miss.** `SPEC-01:193` already contains the rule — *"a baton sentence is not a backlog: when a reviewer routes an item to an existing card, the routing is not complete until it is an explicit DoD bullet on that card, written in the same session"* — written by Dunga after QA-01's F-2, which was **the same failure**. **The rule existed, was correct, and did not fire.** A convention that has now failed twice is not a convention problem; it is a missing gate. Conventions bind the person who remembers them, which is never the person who forgets.

**The amendment (Dunga's ruling, landed on the cards):** every card carries a mandatory **`(z) INTAKE`** DoD bullet — *every* item under `## Debt to hand to Dunga` in that card's review(s) must have an explicit disposition: a **DEBT id**, a **named-card DoD bullet**, or an explicit **`WONTFIX` + reason**. If the heading is absent from a review, the card must say so explicitly. **A card cannot be marked ✅ DONE with `(z)` unticked.** ⛔ Not retro-applied as an un-tick of already-DONE cards; it binds from the next card closed. FU-01.3-I is the worked example, not an exception.

**Why an explicit `WONTFIX` and not silence:** silence is indistinguishable from an oversight, which is precisely the state S-1 spent four hours in. A rejected suggestion with a reason is a decision; an unmentioned one is a leak.

🔗 **Read with [[DEBT-40]]** (mutant population scoped to changed lines), **[[DEBT-43]]** (value obligations not derived from the declared type), and the **PROCESS AMENDMENT** (debt carded by line number). Those three are one failure at three scopes *inside* a review; this is the same failure at the **hand-off between** a review and the backlog.

## DEBT-47 — the declared-type lens is applied to the reported field, not to the type's field set

**Status:** open (process) · **Origin:** QA-01 S-01.3 **re-audit #4**, 2026-09-21 (Zangado) · **Impact: Med-High** — two production defects of the same shape in two days.

> ⚖️ **WAVE-0 AUDIT 2026-09-23 (Dunga) — KEEP OPEN, unchanged. WAVE 2.** Re-verified at HEAD: the destination has not moved. `~/lemon-marketplace/lemon-studio-sdd/skills/code-review-protocol/SKILL.md` was grepped for the clause and for every keyword of it (`__required_keys__`, `get_type_hints`, declared type, reviewer independence) — **no match**. So the rule is still owed to shared plugin tooling this repo cannot land unilaterally, and this row is still the in-repo handle. Nothing in `src/` is owed: the instances remain closed (see the DEBT-48 audit block above — eight of nine, FO-6 the exception, and FO-6 is not a declared-type-lens miss). Land as ONE amendment with DEBT-40 / 43 / 44 / 46 (prose).

> ⚖️ **DISPOSITION 2026-09-22 (Dunga triage) — KEEP OPEN. Owner: Dunga (tracking handle) → the same single `code-review-protocol` amendment as DEBT-40/43/44/46. Next action: no in-repo work remains for the *instances*; the row stays open only as the handle for the protocol clause.**
> **Why it is not resolved even though its known instances are fixed.** Every fail-open instance the lens was pointed at is closed (DEBT-48's sweep: eight of nine, FO-9 closed 2026-09-22 in `859d445`), so there is nothing to fix in `src/`. What remains open is the *rule*, and the rule is owed to shared plugin tooling this repo cannot land unilaterally — the identical constraint as DEBT-31/40.
> ⚠️ **Today's evidence sharpens the rule rather than discharging it, and it is uncomfortable evidence: the lens has a blind spot it cannot see past.** REG-11 is a fail-open defect on `adapter/normalize.py` — the very module the DEBT-48 sweep singled out as *"thoroughly validated — every declared field checked"*. Both statements are true at once, because the sweep checks values against the **declared type** and the declared type encoded the **wrong wire shape**. A `TypedDict`-derived lens validates that the data matches what we believe; it is structurally incapable of noticing that what we believe is false. **Corollary to add to the protocol clause when it lands:** type-derived validation answers *"is this the shape we declared?"*, never *"is the shape we declared the shape the API sends?"* — the second question is only answerable against a recorded live response (standing rule **SR-1**, `REGRESSIONS.md`). Recorded here because it is the fifth scope of this family and the first one the family's own instrument could never have found.

**The observation, verbatim from the audit:** *"Two consecutive audits, two '1 of 2 `str` fields' misses on the same card family, under three reviewer instances. The lens is finding **the reported field**, not **the declared type's field set**. DEBT-40's method is right; it is being applied to the field named in the finding rather than to the TypedDict."*

**The evidence is a matched pair.** Re-audit #3's F-1: `_require_record_shape` value-checked `item_id` and not `document_id` — 1 of 2 `str` fields on `DocumentRecord` (**fail-open**). Re-audit #4's F-1: `get_dataset` value-checks `document_id` and not `item_id` — 1 of 2 `str` fields on `DatasetItem` (fail-closed). **The second landed inside the fix for the first**, written by an implementer who had just been told to derive obligations from the type, and survived three reviewer instances.

**The rule:** when a finding names a field, the reviewer's unit of work is **the declaring type's full field set at every boundary the card names** — `typing.get_type_hints(T)` — never the single field in the finding. A guard that validates *n−1* of *n* fields is the default outcome of hand-enumeration, and the coincidence that makes it invisible is that most of these types have exactly two `str` fields.

🔗 Fourth scope of the **[[DEBT-40]]** / **[[DEBT-43]]** / PROCESS-AMENDMENT family; folds into the same shared `code-review-protocol` amendment, which now carries four arguments rather than three.

---

> ⏰ **DEBT-45 — hard deadline recorded (QA-01 re-audit #4, Zangado).** `Secrets: ⚠️` has now stood for **two consecutive audits**. Ruling: acceptable for S-01.3 to stay Done **for one more audit, not indefinitely**, on the predicate that (a) the working tree is clean, (b) the historical hit is triaged and verified non-credential, and (c) the fingerprint entry is a **human** decision blocked on nobody but the human — *"and that is the part that must not drift."* **If DEBT-45 is still open at S-01.4's `/qa`, it is an S-01.4 Done-blocker.** S-01.3 is not the story whose Done depends on the secret gate's definition; **T-01.4.10 / S-01.4 is.** It is a five-line `.gitleaks.toml` fingerprint entry.

So the method was applied **once, systematically**: every declared structured type in `src/` (26 types across 23 files), every field, every trust-boundary crossing, classified VALIDATED / UNVALIDATED-fail-closed / **UNVALIDATED-fail-open**.

| **FO-8** | `ExperimentItem.dataset_id` (declared `Any`; the SDK's contract is a string id) from `body.get("id")` with no check → every item can carry `None`, producing an experiment with no dataset linkage while the structural check passes | S-01.3 | ✅ **CLOSED 2026-09-21** (`8455a39`) — ⏭️ **CARD.** This is the ADR-0005 #9 failure mode (a run invisible/unlinked in the Experiments tab) reported as a clean run |
| **FO-6** | `Dataset.expected_output_schema` returned verbatim, no `isinstance` — a platform body carrying a string/list yields a `Dataset` violating its own declared type | S-01.4 | ⏭️ **CARD (latent).** Consumer is the orchestrator's `schema_drift` comparison (ADR-0005 #8), which does not exist yet |
| **FO-9** | `LANGFUSE_HOST` (and `IDP_REGION`/`IDP_ORG_ID`) have no scheme/format validation; credentials are sent to whatever host the env names. **Note the asymmetry:** the same module fails closed on a `LANGFUSE_BASE_URL` split-brain and both transports refuse cross-host redirects — the env path is the one unguarded leg of that same threat | S-01.4 / T-01.4.10 | ⏭️ **CARD** alongside **DEBT-45**; same threat family (a credential reaching a host the caller did not choose — REG-07/REG-10's class) |

**Common shape, worth stating once:** six of the nine are **a declaration written and never enforced** — three `Literal`s (`RunStatus`, `gate`, `VerdictLiteral`) and several `str`s that exist only as type hints. `mypy` cannot catch them because the values arrive at runtime from an API, an env var, or a caller; the type is documentation that nothing reads. That is the generalisation of **DEBT-43**, and it is why the `_str_fields`/`get_args` mechanism matters beyond the one guard it was built for.

**What the sweep also showed, and it is not all bad news:** `normalize()` (`adapter/normalize.py`) and the IDP OAuth/submit/poll path (`adapter/idp_client.py`) are **thoroughly validated** — every declared field checked, allowlists held as config rather than literals, timing config range-checked at construction *and* at env-parse. The gaps are oversight in one package, not an absence of rigour in the project.

## 🚧 DEBT-45 — BLOCKED ON A HUMAN DECISION (recorded 2026-09-21)

> ⚖️ **WAVE-0 AUDIT 2026-09-23 (Dunga) — ✅ NO LONGER BLOCKED. The human decision was taken and the redefinition SHIPPED; this section is now historical.** Verified at HEAD `49939b2`: `.gitleaksignore` carries exactly the one fingerprint drafted below (not a `paths` blind); `.github/workflows/secrets-gate.yml`'s `gitleaks-diff` job is genuinely **merge-base scoped** (`BASE="$(git merge-base origin/$BASE_REF HEAD)"`, `--log-opts="${BASE}..HEAD"`); `.githooks/pre-commit` runs `gitleaks git --staged .`; the `env-hygiene` job asserts `.env` ignored and untracked; and full history moved to the **separate scheduled** `secrets-audit.yml` with `continue-on-error: true` — exactly the three-way split ruled for below. Live run at HEAD: `gitleaks git . --redact` → **no leaks found**, exit 0, 267 commits scanned. The Zangado deadline (*"if DEBT-45 is still open at S-01.4's `/qa`, it is an S-01.4 Done-blocker"`*) is satisfied, not waived. See the struck table row `~~DEBT-45~~`.

**This is the only item in the current set that an agent cannot close.** It is not blocked on effort, analysis or sequencing — it is blocked on a person choosing a security posture. Recorded here in a state where that choice is one word.

**The situation.** `gitleaks git .` reports **1** leak over full history: a **test-fixture literal** at `tests/platform/test_langfuse_adapter.py:305`, committed in `353549d`, renamed in the working tree by FU-01.3-D DoD (h) but **immutable in history**. Verified across three audits: it matches **no** value in the gitignored `.env`, and `.env` is untracked. **Not a credential; no rotation required.** The working tree and staged scans are clean.

**Why it cannot be closed by working harder.** DoD (h)'s exit condition (*"re-run `gitleaks git .` and confirm 0 leaks"*) is **unsatisfiable without rewriting published history**. Atchim ruled **T-01.4.10 leg 1's blocking-full-history definition is itself the defect**, on four counts: its verdict depends on the past rather than the change under review; any historical finding is permanently un-closeable except by a destructive operation (*"a control whose only remedy is a destructive operation is not a control"*); it is O(history) on every PR; and it conflates *"does this PR introduce a secret"* with *"does history contain one"*, whose answer is **rotation**, not a red build.

⚠️ **And the obvious fix does not work.** Zangado tested the recommended **merge-base scoping** and it **does not clear this branch**: merge-base is `d3d1444`, **129 commits back**, so `353549d` sits *inside this PR's own range*. A diff-scoped blocking gate goes red exactly like the full-history one. **The fingerprint entry is therefore load-bearing, not a nicety.**

### The decision, with the mechanism already verified

⚠️ **Correction to the earlier recommendation, found by dry-running it (orchestrator, 2026-09-21):** the proposed `.gitleaks.toml` `[allowlist] fingerprints = [...]` **fails to load** on gitleaks 8.30.1 — `"[[allowlists]] must contain at least one check for: commits, paths, regexes, or stopwords"`. Fingerprints are **not** a `.gitleaks.toml` feature in this version. **The working mechanism is a `.gitleaksignore` file**, tested end-to-end: full history → `no leaks found`, and `gitleaks protect --staged` → still armed. Had the earlier five-line recommendation been applied as written, the gate would have been silently broken rather than fixed.

**Ready to apply — `.gitleaksignore` at the repo root:**
```
# DEBT-45 — one triaged historical test-fixture literal.
# Committed in 353549d, renamed in the working tree by FU-01.3-D DoD (h)
# but immutable in history. Verified NOT a credential: matches no value
# in the gitignored .env, which is untracked. No rotation required.
# A fingerprint pins exactly one commit x file x line x rule — it cannot
# blind a future commit, another line, or the same line in a later commit.
# DO NOT replace with a tests/ path allowlist.
353549d18af0f2128e5323d104b42a5bf292fcb0:tests/platform/test_langfuse_adapter.py:generic-api-key:305
```

**Also needs deciding (T-01.4.10 leg 1's definition):** blocking gate scoped to **merge-base…HEAD** *plus* `gitleaks protect --staged` at pre-commit, with **full-history as a separate, non-blocking scheduled audit** against this triaged baseline. ⛔ **Never** a `paths = ['''tests/''']` allowlist — both reviewers were emphatic that it blinds the gate on the highest-risk file class in the repo, since a captured fixture is exactly how a real value gets committed by accident. ⛔ **No history rewriting.** Squash-merge is a side effect of a merge mode, **not a control** — it does not survive a cherry-pick, a fork, or a clone that already has `353549d`.

### Deadline (Zangado, re-audit #4)
`Secrets: ⚠️` has stood for **two consecutive audits**. Acceptable for S-01.3 to stay Done **for one more audit, not indefinitely**. **If DEBT-45 is still open at S-01.4's `/qa`, it is an S-01.4 Done-blocker** — S-01.3 is not the story whose Done depends on the secret gate's definition; **T-01.4.10 / S-01.4 is.**

## 🔴 DEBT-49 — `TypedDict.__required_keys__` is UNSOUND in this codebase, and DEBT-43's rule half-rests on it

**Status:** open · **Origin:** FU/FO-2 implementation, 2026-09-21 (found by the implementer, **independently verified by the orchestrator**) · **Impact: High** — it invalidates half of a rule this project has been building on all session, and it is a latent production regression, not only a process defect.

> ⚖️ **WAVE-0 AUDIT 2026-09-23 (Dunga) — ✅ ALREADY-CLOSED. All three required follow-ups have landed; the row's `Status: open` was stale.**
> 1. ✅ `__required_keys__` is gone from every derivation path: `grep -rn "__required_keys__" src/ tests/` returns **docstrings and the deliberate M13 drift pin only** — no live use. `langfuse_adapter.py:114-115` is now `get_type_hints(td, include_extras=True)` filtered on `get_origin(hint) is not NotRequired`, mirrored test-side at `tests/platform/_type_pins.py:38`.
> 2. ✅ DEBT-43's rule text was amended (by DEBT-54's close, 2026-09-22) to prescribe the sound derivation.
> 3. ✅ The M13 pin exists and passes: `test_m13_drift_pin_scoreinput_required_keys_is_unsound_today` (`tests/platform/test_record_run_preconditions.py:611`), plus a production-side twin at `:875`.
> ⚠️ **This closure is for the PRESENCE half only and must not be read as closing the family.** The VALUE-TYPE half is still unsound and is now measured — see **DEBT-53 (prose)** immediately below, which this audit re-verified empirically. Per the closure-wording rule at `## PROCESS AMENDMENT`: a closed bullet is not a closed class.

**Measured, not inferred** (Python 3.13.5, `from __future__ import annotations` in `platform/types.py`):
```
ScoreInput:     required=['comment', 'id', 'name', 'value']   optional=[]
DocumentRecord: required=['document_id', 'item_id', 'scores'] optional=[]
DatasetItem:    required=['document_id', 'golden', 'item_id'] optional=[]
```
`ScoreInput.comment` is declared **`NotRequired[str | None]`** and appears in **`__required_keys__`**; `__optional_keys__` is **empty**. Under postponed evaluation of annotations the annotation is a *string* at class-creation time, so `NotRequired` is never detected. **`__required_keys__` does not mean what it says here.**

**Why this matters beyond one guard.** **[[DEBT-43]]** states the rule this session has been applying everywhere: *"for every field the guard protects, derive BOTH obligations mechanically — presence from `__required_keys__`, value type from `typing.get_type_hints(T)`."* **The presence half is unsound.** It has been producing correct results **by luck**: `DocumentRecord` and `DatasetItem` happen to have no `NotRequired` fields. `ScoreInput` does, and it is the first type where the mechanism was pointed at a `NotRequired` field.

⚠️ **The live landmine.** `langfuse_adapter.py:138` still reads `sorted(DocumentRecord.__required_keys__)`, and `test_record_run_preconditions.py:773` still parametrizes over it. **Add a single `NotRequired` field to `DocumentRecord` and the guard immediately starts rejecting records that legitimately omit it** — a production regression, fail-closed but a real outage, arriving from a change that looks entirely safe. The "type-driven ratchet" that FU-01.3-D installed to make schema changes safe would itself be the thing that breaks on the next schema change.

**The sound derivation, and why it works:** `get_type_hints(T)` filtered on `hint is str` (the existing `_str_fields`). `NotRequired[str | None]` resolves to `str | None`, which **is not** `str`, so an optional field is excluded **correctly** — for the right reason, not by coincidence. The FO-2 implementer collapsed presence + type into one loop over that derivation rather than use `__required_keys__`, and mirrored a production-side `_str_annotated_field_names` in `langfuse_adapter.py` (src must not import test code). **That deviation from the brief was correct and is endorsed.**

**Required follow-up (not done here — it is a production change on a guard carrying a live QA stamp):**
1. Replace `__required_keys__` at `langfuse_adapter.py:138` and the parametrize at `test_record_run_preconditions.py:773` with the `get_type_hints`-based derivation, so `DocumentRecord`'s guard stops being correct-by-luck.
2. **Amend DEBT-43's rule**: presence must be derived from `get_type_hints` + a `NotRequired` check that works under postponed annotations (`typing.get_type_hints(T, include_extras=True)` and inspecting for `NotRequired`), **never** from `__required_keys__` while `from __future__ import annotations` is in effect.
3. Add a pin, in the shape of M13: assert `__required_keys__` does **not** match the sound derivation for `ScoreInput`, so if a future Python or typing version fixes the behaviour, the mismatch surfaces deliberately instead of silently changing the guard's meaning.

🔗 This is the **fifth** scope of the [[DEBT-40]] / [[DEBT-43]] / [[DEBT-47]] / PROCESS-AMENDMENT family — and the first where the *instrument itself* was faulty rather than merely aimed too narrowly. Every prior member was "the right tool applied to too small a target"; this one is "the tool reports the wrong answer and nothing noticed, because the two types it was first pointed at had no optional fields."

> ### 📊 DEBT-48 sweep status, 2026-09-21 end of session
> **Closed: FO-1, FO-2, FO-3, FO-4, FO-5, FO-7 (all three legs), FO-8** — **seven** of nine, plus **DEBT-49**, the unsound-instrument finding that came out of FO-2 and that every other type-driven guard in the repo rested on.
> ⚠️ **Correction (2026-09-21, found by an independent gate):** this line previously read "six of nine" and omitted **FO-8**, which was fixed in `8455a39` — its own commit title says `#FO-8` and the gate verified it live (mutants MY-4/MY-5 killed, including the compound `or`→`and` the implementer had honestly flagged as untested). An **underclaim** rather than an overclaim, but it is exactly how closed work gets re-done.
> **Remaining: FO-6** (`expected_output_schema` — latent; its consumer, the orchestrator's `schema_drift` comparison, does not exist until S-01.4) and **FO-9** (`LANGFUSE_HOST`/`IDP_REGION`/`IDP_ORG_ID` have no scheme or format validation — same threat family as **DEBT-45**/REG-07/REG-10, and best decided together with the T-01.4.10 gate definition the human still owns).
> ⚠️ **Both stamps are now STALE.** `classifier/gate.py` (FO-5) stales **S-01.1**; `platform/langfuse_adapter.py` + `scoring.py` stale **S-01.3**. Neither story's Done is currently backed by a fresh stamp, and `/qa` will block on freshness for both. The re-stamps must be run under **DEBT-44** by an instance that did not review these diffs.
