## ~~DEBT-50~~ — T-01.4.4 hides S-01.2 work behind an S-01.4 task number — ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-09-22 (overtaken by events), one residual carried as a proposed spec delta**

**Status:** ~~open (scope/planning)~~ ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-09-22 (Dunga triage)** · **Origin:** S-01.4 estimation pass, 2026-09-21 (Dengoso) · **Impact: Med** — it misattributes a story's cost and hides a cross-story re-stamp.

> ⚖️ **DISPOSITION — RESOLVED, and resolved by the thing happening rather than by the thing being prevented. Recorded honestly, because "resolved" here does not mean "the row was wrong".**
> This row asked for one preventive action: *annotate T-01.4.4 with its true file set and the S-01.2 re-stamp **before S-01.4 is scheduled***. That window closed — **the work has since shipped**. `22a4712` (*"bounded poll retry (5xx/429) + mid-poll 401/403 refresh-retry"*) landed the refresh-then-retry in **`src/idp_regression/adapter/idp_client.py`**, exactly where DEBT-21 said it had to live, under S-01.4's task number, and `idp_client.py:332-361` now carries the mid-poll 401/403 seam with a docstring that says so.
> **The predicted consequence partly materialised, which is the row's vindication, not its refutation.** S-01.2's stamp did go stale on adapter work carried under another story's ledger: it was re-gated at `99101ac` (2026-09-22, DEBT-46 close-out), then **staled again the same day** by `6755fa1` (poll `?valueOnly=false` + the 10 s interval floor) and `0292d0e` (REG-11). An Atchim review of the REG-11 delta is in flight (W1.1) and a `/test` re-stamp by a **different instance** is still owed under **DEBT-44**, with `Risk: high` binding regardless of the `prototype` profile. That re-stamp is tracked on the plan, not here — this row is not the handle for it.
> **Residual, carried as a PROPOSED DELTA because `docs/specs/` is out of this triage's write scope — owner: Dunga, on the next SPEC-01 touch.** `SPEC-01:380` and the estimate note at `:603` still describe T-01.4.4 without naming its true file set. Proposed wording: *"T-01.4.4 — 401 mid-run refresh-then-fail-closed. **Cross-story: the refresh-then-retry is an `adapter/` change (S-01.2 file set: `src/idp_regression/adapter/idp_client.py`) and carries an S-01.2 `/test` re-stamp; only the `auth_failure` mapping layer is S-01.4's.** Landed 22a4712."* **Do not silently re-file the points to S-01.2** — the split is genuine and the 5 points were estimated against the split; what was missing was visibility, and visibility is what the annotation buys.

T-01.4.4 ("401 mid-run refresh-then-fail-closed", **5 pts**, carried on S-01.4's ledger) is mostly **S-01.2** work. Its own DoD says so: per **DEBT-21**, once `extract()` has raised, the orchestrator cannot retry the triggering poll without an illegal re-submit — so the refresh-then-retry has to live in **the adapter's poll loop**. QA S-01.2 F-5's submit-401/403 → `auth_failure` mis-mapping rides along with it.

So the 5 points cover an **S-01.2 module change + an S-01.2 `/test` re-stamp + an S-01.4 mapping layer**, while appearing on S-01.4's ledger as orchestrator work. The mapping layer genuinely *is* S-01.4's, so this is not necessarily a re-file — but the **cross-story file set and the second re-stamp must be visible before the work starts**, not discovered when S-01.2's stamp goes stale mid-S-01.4.

This is the same "wrong story's debt" shape Dunga ruled against for FU-01.1-A, and the same reason FU-01.3-G was kept off the test-hygiene card FU-01.3-F.

**Action:** annotate T-01.4.4 with its true file set and the S-01.2 re-stamp before S-01.4 is scheduled; decide deliberately whether the adapter half re-files to S-01.2 or stays as declared cross-story work.

## ~~DEBT-51~~ — WITHDRAWN: DEBT-30 is singly owned; the finding was a misreading

> ⚖️ **WAVE-0 AUDIT 2026-09-23 (Dunga) — ✅ CONFIRMED WITHDRAWN; the `**Status:** open` line at the top of this section is stale and contradicts the heading, which already reads `~~DEBT-51~~ — WITHDRAWN`.** Re-verified: DEBT-30's duty is singly owned and is now *discharged* by `orchestration/bootstrap.py:55-84` (`validate_platform_credentials`, called at `facade.py:385` before `make_platform` at `:406`), so neither the double-ownership this row alleged nor the duty it worried about is live. Kept, not deleted, per this section's own reasoning. ⚠️ **Do not confuse this with the TABLE row `DEBT-51`** (`mypy tests` is in no gate), which this audit dispositioned **already-closed** on different evidence (`quality-gate.yml`). Two unrelated items, one number — the hazard the header warning describes.

**Status:** open (scope/planning) · **Origin:** S-01.4 estimation pass, 2026-09-21 (Dengoso) · **Impact: Low** — a double-count, but the failure mode is worse than the points.

**DEBT-30** (`make_platform` raises a bare `KeyError` on a missing env var, so NFR **N6**'s "fail closed with a clear message" clause is unmet) is referenced under **both** `T-01.4.1`'s fail-closed-credential DoD bullet **and** `T-01.4.10`'s hygiene surface.

The real risk is not the ~1 point of double-counting. It is the **opposite** outcome: a duty owned by two tasks is a duty each can assume the other discharged, which is how N6 reaches `/qa` unmet with both tasks ticked. That is the mirror image of DEBT-46 — there, a routed item had no owner; here it has two, which fails the same way.

⚠️ **WITHDRAWN 2026-09-21 — this finding was wrong, and I verified it rather than acting on it.** The double-ownership does not exist in the authoritative records:
- `DEBT-30`'s own row names **only `T-01.4.1`**.
- `T-01.4.10`'s task text covers **N4, N5, N17, INV-02, INV-05** — **not N6**, and does not mention the `KeyError` wrap.
- **`SPEC-01` never references `DEBT-30` at all**, under either task.
- The S-01.4 compliance DoD bullet at `:356` covers credential hygiene and the gitleaks gate; it carries no N6 or `KeyError` duty.

The claim came from an estimation pass inferring shared ownership from adjacent prose. **No action needed — the duty is already singly owned by T-01.4.1.** Kept as a withdrawn row rather than deleted, because a finding that was recorded and then silently vanished is the [[DEBT-46]] failure in reverse: the next reader would find the S-01.4 kickoff telling them to 'settle DEBT-51 before starting' and no trace of why it stopped mattering.

**The reusable lesson is the same one this session keeps re-learning:** a finding is a hypothesis until checked against the authoritative record. I recorded this one on a subagent's reading without verifying it, which is the same shape as the five overclaims — a claim written one notch wider than the evidence.

## 📌 BACKLOG-01 — Automatic trigger: fire validation after a new prompt executes at IDP

**Status:** open, **investigation carded (T-01.6.6), nothing designed or built** · **Origin:** user requirement, 2026-09-21 · **Assumption:** [[ASM-06]] · **Scope: Epic-level, post-UC-01 — explicitly NOT MVP**

> *"We need to find a way to trigger the validation after a new prompt completes its execution at IDP."*

**The gap, stated without softening.** Today nothing triggers anything. The system is a **pull-based batch runner** started by a human or by CI on a prompt-change PR. There are **no webhooks, events or subscriptions anywhere in the design** — verified by grep across `docs/adr/` and `docs/design/`, 2026-09-21. **So the safety net only catches prompt changes that go through a PR.** A version published and executed directly in Anypoint is invisible.

**What is NOT the gap — worth stating, because it is the intuitive worry and it is already solved.** There is no latency or hand-off problem between IDP and the scoring half. `IDPClient.extract()` is `submit` → `poll until terminal status`; the moment it returns, `classify()` runs on that output **in the same call stack**, then `overall_gate()`, then `build_score_inputs()`, then one batched `record_run()`. The numbers are produced milliseconds after the execution completes. The coupling is **temporal, not architectural** — no callback from IDP is needed for *that* half. The only missing piece there is the loop itself, which is **S-01.4** (`orchestration/` contains only `__init__.py`), already designed and estimated at 49 pts.

**So the ask reduces to exactly one thing: what starts `run_eval`.**

**Half the mechanism already exists.** Every run records `action_version` into `RunMetadata` (**INV-04**), so *"what did we last certify?"* is already a query against Langfuse. Only *"what is published / what just ran?"* is missing — and that needs an Anypoint **management-plane** API this app has never called. The single endpoint it knows is on `idp-rt.{region}` (the **runtime** plane). **The shape and even the existence of a listing or notification API are unverified and were deliberately not guessed at.**

**Investigation — carded as `T-01.6.6`, discovery only, inside S-01.6's existing live-access window:**
1. Is there a management API that **lists an action's versions**, and what does it return (ids, timestamps, published/draft flag)?
2. Does submitting support a **floating/`latest`** version? *(If yes, detection is nearly free: submit one document, read back which version answered, compare to the last certified `action_version` — no listing API required.)*
3. Does a published version carry a **timestamp or monotonic ordering** comparable to a previously recorded one?
4. **Is there any push mechanism at all** — Anypoint notifications, CloudHub/Runtime Manager alerts, an event stream? *(If a push exists, it beats all polling designs and changes the answer entirely.)*

**⚠️ The cost asymmetry that decides the design.** A listing call is **one cheap metadata request**; a regression run costs **N document extractions** of real IDP quota and money. "Just run it nightly" therefore detects with the expensive instrument what a metadata call would catch cheaply — *unless* listing does not exist, in which case a low-frequency scheduled run may genuinely be the only option. The answers decide between:

| Outcome | Design |
|---|---|
| Push mechanism exists | **Event-driven** — strictly best, no polling, no quota burn |
| Listing API exists | **Cheap metadata watcher** — poll versions, run the regression only on change |
| Floating `latest` exists | **Near-free detection** — one probe extraction reveals the live version |
| None of the above | **Scheduled full run**, low frequency, priced against quota — or stay with CI-on-PR |

**⚠️ Ambiguity the user must resolve before any design work.** *"After a new prompt completes its execution"* reads two ways, and they are **different products**:
- **(a) A new action *version* was published and run** → re-run the golden set against it. **This product.** The watcher above serves it.
- **(b) A *production document* was extracted** → judge that output live. **NOT this product.** The classifier compares an extraction against a **known-good golden for that specific document**; an arbitrary production document has none, so there is nothing to compare against. That is drift/confidence monitoring — a different Epic with a different data model. Building (b) is not an extension of this system; it is a new one.

**⚠️ Scope fence.** Nothing here is MVP. The published IDP action id is already the **only** critical path; this must not become a second one. `T-01.6.6` adds *questions* answerable while the org is already open — it adds no build work and does not extend S-01.6's half-day time-box.

---

## 🔴 DEBT-52 — `git add -A` while a subagent is working: two mislabelled commits, one shipped mutant

**Status:** rule adopted, history left intact · **Origin:** orchestrator error, 2026-09-21, twice in one session · **Impact: High** — one instance silently reverted a correctness fix and shipped an actively-applied mutant.

> ⚖️ **WAVE-0 AUDIT 2026-09-23 (Dunga) — NOT OPEN WORK. The rule is adopted and it held.** This audit staged explicit paths only (`docs/state/DEBT.md`, `docs/state/PROGRESS.md`) while a concurrent agent was writing in `src/idp_regression/orchestration/` (two commits landed under it mid-audit, `49939b2` and `bf3870b`). History remains unrewritten, as ruled. Kept as the standing record, not counted in the open backlog.

**What happened, twice, both mine.**

| Commit | Claims to be | Actually contained |
|---|---|---|
| `b56f105` | *"spec(S-01.6): add T-01.6.6"* | the spec change **+ a 1-line revert of `include_extras=True` in `langfuse_adapter.py`** (half of DEBT-49's own fix) **+ 44 lines of a test file** |
| `dd3ad51` | *"docs(backlog): record BACKLOG-01 + ASM-06"* | the docs **+ the entire Required-A/B fix round** — 81 lines of `langfuse_adapter.py`, 151 of `test_record_run_preconditions.py`, 11 of `test_langfuse_adapter.py` |

**Cause, precisely.** `git add -A` stages the whole working tree. A subagent was concurrently editing those files, and in `b56f105`'s case was **mid-mutation-test** — apply mutant → observe → restore. The `add -A` landed between *apply* and *restore*, so **an actively-applied mutant was committed as if it were source.** The mutant was exactly "drop `include_extras=True`", i.e. Required A's mutation 3, which is why it reproduced later against what looked like clean HEAD.

**Why it shipped silently:** the pin that would have caught it did not exist yet — it was the very thing Required A was raised to add. With the new probe pin in place, re-applying that change fails `test_production_required_field_names_matches_the_sound_derivation_on_a_probe_type` (verified). The defect and its detector were created in the same round, in that order.

**The rule, adopted now:**
> **Never `git add -A` / `git commit -a` while any subagent has write access to the repo.** Stage explicit paths only — `git add docs/state/DEBT.md`, never `git add -A`. A concurrent agent's working tree is not a stable snapshot: it may hold an applied mutant, a half-finished edit, or a deliberately-broken RED state.

**Second-order lesson, which is the more general one.** Both commits *appeared* clean — tests passed, `mypy`/`ruff` were clean — because the swallowed content was either a valid intermediate state or a mutant whose detector did not yet exist. **A green suite does not tell you what is in your commit.** `git show --stat` before committing is the check; the file list is the only thing that reveals a docs commit carrying production source.

**History deliberately NOT rewritten.** The branch's commits are wrong in their *messages*, not in their *content* — the final tree is correct (559 passed, `include_extras=True` restored, pins in place). Rewriting published history to fix a message is the destructive remedy this project already rejected for DEBT-45's gitleaks hit, and the same reasoning applies. This row is the record; the commits stay.

🔗 Sibling of [[DEBT-46]] (a routed item with no owner) and [[DEBT-51]] (a duty with two owners). All three are *bookkeeping that diverged from reality* — and this one is the sharpest, because the divergence was introduced by the person writing the record.

---

## 🔴 DEBT-53 — `NotRequired[str]` is conflated with required `str`: DEBT-49's landmine, inside DEBT-49's own fix

**Status:** open, **latent — no current field triggers it** · **Origin:** QA audit F-2, 2026-09-21 (Zangado) · **Verified independently by the orchestrator** · **Impact: High when it fires**

> ⚖️ **WAVE-0 AUDIT 2026-09-23 (Dunga) — STILL OPEN, and now MEASURED at HEAD rather than argued. LANE C.**
> Run at HEAD `49939b2`, both derivations, against the repo's own probe type:
> ```
> _str_fields(_StrFieldProbe)                    -> ['field_a', 'field_b', 'field_c', 'optional_field']
> _str_annotated_field_names(_StrFieldProbe)     -> ['field_a', 'field_b', 'field_c', 'optional_field']
> ```
> `optional_field` is declared **`NotRequired[str]`** (`tests/platform/_type_pins.py:73`) and is **wrongly included by both**, exactly as this row predicted — `langfuse_adapter.py:73` and `_type_pins.py:56` still call `get_type_hints(td)` **without** `include_extras=True`. The **presence** half was fixed (`_required_field_names`, `:114-115`, correctly excludes `NotRequired`); the **value-type** half was not. The "seventh overclaim" docstring (*"sidesteps that bug entirely"*) is still live at `langfuse_adapter.py:66-72`.
> `_require_record_shape`'s score loop still combines presence+type over `_SCORE_STR_FIELDS` and its own comment concedes the soundness condition: *"sound here only because every one of `ScoreInput`'s REQUIRED fields … is also `str`-annotated"*. **F-5 also still open:** `mark_run_status` hand-writes `Literal["aborted", "complete"]` at `langfuse_adapter.py:823` four lines under the "never hand-written" comment, while `_VALID_RUN_STATUSES` (`:124`) derives from `RunStatus`.
> **A fix must:** derive with `include_extras=True`, treat `get_origin(hint) is NotRequired` as optional-typed (type-check only *if present*), split the score loop the way the metadata loop is split, take the one-token F-5 fix, and pin it with the probe that already carries a `NotRequired[str]` field — the probe exists; only the assertion is missing.

**Measured**, not inferred:
```python
class Probe(TypedDict):
    a: str
    b: NotRequired[str]        # optional
    c: NotRequired[str | None]
    d: int

_str_annotated_field_names(Probe) -> ['a', 'b']   # ⚠️ 'b' wrongly included
```
`_str_annotated_field_names` (`langfuse_adapter.py`) and its test twin `_str_fields` (`tests/platform/_type_pins.py`) call `get_type_hints(td)` **without `include_extras=True`**, so `NotRequired[str]` collapses to plain `str` and is treated as a **required** obligation.

**Two consumers conflate that list with presence:**
- `_require_record_shape`'s score loop — a future `NotRequired[str]` on `ScoreInput` makes the guard **reject every score that legitimately omits it.** That is **DEBT-49's "live landmine" shape verbatim**, reproduced *inside the fix written to eliminate it*.
- `_require_run_metadata_shape`'s value loop subscripts unguarded — a future `NotRequired[str]` on `RunMetadata` escapes as a bare **`KeyError`** (REG-09's untyped-escape family).

**Why nothing fires today, and why that is not reassuring.** `ScoreInput.comment` is `NotRequired[str | None]`, which resolves to `str | None` — not `str` — so it is excluded **by luck, not by design**. The moment anyone writes `NotRequired[str]` (no `| None`) on either type, the guard breaks.

**Seventh overclaim, in production source:** the docstring claiming this derivation *"sidesteps that bug entirely"* is false. It sidesteps it only for the annotation shapes that happen to exist right now.

**Fix (with F-5 folded in):** derive with `include_extras=True`; treat `get_origin(hint) is NotRequired` as **optional-typed** (type-check only *if present*); split the score loop the way the metadata loop now is; pin it with a probe carrying a **`NotRequired[str]`** field driven through a shared `_require_shape(td, value)` — the debt the stamp already carries. Also **F-5**: `mark_run_status` hand-writes `status: Literal["aborted","complete"]` four lines under a comment saying "never hand-written", while `_VALID_RUN_STATUSES` derives from `types.RunStatus`. One-token fix. **Atchim ruling: widen REG-09.**

⚠️ **This is the sixth member of the DEBT-40/43/47/49/52 family, and the most pointed:** the previous record was "fixed one guard, reintroduced it one guard over." This one is *fixed the instrument, and the replacement instrument has the same flaw on a different axis.*

## ~~DEBT-55 (prose)~~ — the `prototype` profile has no matrix — ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-09-25**

> ✅ **CLOSED 2026-09-25 (user decision, both halves answered in one pass).**
> **(a) The level:** the user re-confirmed `prototype`. Recorded, not silently applied.
> **(b) The matrix:** the draft below was **adopted verbatim into `CLAUDE.md ## Rigor`**, where
> `/qa` reads it. The invariant *"every gate the profile turns off is recorded, never dropped"* is
> now mechanically checkable, which is the whole of what this row asked for.
> **What the adoption made explicit, and is worth carrying:** the matrix exempts **nothing that
> touches `src/`**, so `prototype` buys exactly one reviewer pass on docs/tests-only deltas. The
> escalation this row carried (re-raise to `standard`?) is therefore **answered in substance
> rather than declined** — the paths that mattered were never profile-driven to begin with.
> **One gap was found while adopting it and is carried forward, NOT closed here:** `SPEC-01` is
> the only spec in the repo, so Epic E's console — the one surface that spends IDP quota from an
> unauthenticated loopback port — has **no SPEC header** for the `Risk: high` row to read. Filed
> as a Step-0 row in `PLAN-DEBT-2026-09-25.md`; it is a gate with nothing to read, not a gate the
> profile lightened.

**Status:** ✅ resolved 2026-09-25 · *prior status, preserved verbatim:* open (process) · **Origin:** QA audit F-6, 2026-09-21 · **Impact: Med** ⬆️ **raised to High 2026-09-22 — see the disposition.**

> ⚖️ **WAVE-0 AUDIT 2026-09-23 (Dunga) — STILL OPEN, unchanged, and the profile half is STILL THE USER'S. Surfaced, not decided.**
> Re-verified at HEAD `49939b2`: `CLAUDE.md ## Rigor` still names `prototype` and still defines **no matrix**, so the invariant *"every gate the profile turns off is recorded, never dropped"* is still mechanically unenforceable. The draft matrix below is unchanged and still ready to adopt.
> 🔺 **The escalation stands and is now one day older.** Should `## Rigor` be re-raised `prototype` → `standard` now, rather than at the "before this tool gates another team" trigger already written in `CLAUDE.md`? **Dunga does not decide this and has not acted on it; `CLAUDE.md` is untouched by this audit.** What today's audit adds to the evidence, and it cuts both ways: (a) **for** — nine commits landed on 2026-09-23 with **zero** review artifacts (DEBT-44, and new **DEBT-72**: S-01.4's stamp is stale and nobody was tracking it), which is what a lighter profile looks like in practice; (b) **against** — of the 20 rows this audit found already closed, none were closed *because* of a profile-driven gate, and the two defect classes still beating this project (wire-contract blindness, DEBT-69; and un-enforced declarations, DEBT-48 prose) are **orthogonal to profile level** — `standard` would not have caught either. **The honest reading is unchanged: the profile is unfalsifiable as a control until the matrix exists.** Write the matrix (Dunga + Zangado); put the level in front of the user before the next `/qa`.

> ⚖️ **DISPOSITION 2026-09-22 (Dunga triage) — KEEP OPEN, IMPACT RAISED Med → High. Owner of the matrix: Dunga + Zangado. Owner of the profile level itself: THE USER — surfaced below with evidence, explicitly not decided here. Next action: write the matrix (draft below, ready to adopt), and put the profile question in front of the user before the next `/qa`.**
>
> **The evidence, stated plainly, because `CLAUDE.md ## Rigor` predicted it in writing and the prediction came true within a day.** That section warns that a lighter profile is *least* likely to catch fail-open defects producing silently-wrong GREEN builds, and names them the worst thing this system can emit. On **2026-09-22** two more fail-open defects reached a story that was **Done, freshly stamped (`99101ac`), and 886 tests green**: REG-11 **D1** (`normalize()` returning an empty `SUCCEEDED` for every real IDP body) and **D2** (every real `confidenceScore` silently becoming `None`). **Neither was found by the suite. Both were found by one live API call.** That is now **four** fail-open defects in two days (2026-09-21: `overall_gate` falling through to `"PASS"`, and a non-`str` `document_id` written to the platform on a successful return; 2026-09-22: D1 and D2), two of them on the profile's own watch.
> ⚠️ **What that evidence does and does not prove — do not overstate it, the register has been burned by exactly this.** It proves the **matrix gap is real and costly**: nobody can say which gate, if any, would have caught D1/D2, because no document states what `prototype` turns off. It does **not** prove `standard` would have caught them — D1 was invisible to *every* profile, since every adapter fixture was authored from the parser (DEBT-69) and no amount of reviewer passes falsifies a suite that can only agree with itself. **The honest reading: the profile is unfalsifiable as a control until the matrix exists, and the defect class that is beating us is orthogonal to profile level.** Both findings belong in front of the user; neither is a reason to decide for them.
> 🔺 **ESCALATION TO THE USER (decision, not a recommendation to act on without them): should `## Rigor` be re-raised from `prototype` to `standard` now, rather than at the "before this tool gates another team" trigger already written there?** Arguments for: four fail-open defects in two days, two of them post-change; the product's entire value is being a gate other teams trust. Arguments against: the trigger condition has not been reached (nothing external consumes this gate yet), and the MVP-speed reason the user gave on 2026-09-21 still holds. **Dunga does not decide this and has not acted on it.** `CLAUDE.md` is unchanged by this triage.
>
> **Draft matrix — ready to adopt, written so the invariant *"every gate the profile turns off is recorded, never dropped"* becomes mechanically checkable.** It is deliberately expressed as *what changes*, per path:
> | Path | `prototype` | `standard` | Never profile-driven |
> |---|---|---|---|
> | **Verdict path** — `classifier/gate.py` (`overall_gate`), `platform/scoring.py` (`build_score_inputs`'s gate value) | **runs at `full` anyway** — fresh-instance stamp with a mutation matrix, no `re-gate skipped` | same | — |
> | **Wire-contract path** — `adapter/` code whose correctness depends on an external API's shape | **SR-1 binds** (see `REGRESSIONS.md`): verified against a recorded live response, never against a fixture authored from the parser | same | — |
> | Any fix round touching `src/` | **never qualifies for `re-gate skipped`, at any profile** | same | — |
> | Reviewer passes on docs/tests-only deltas | one pass | two | — |
> | Mechanical floor (tests exist and pass, mypy, ruff, secret scan) | — | — | **always** |
> | `Risk: high` spec → `/test` stamp; DEBT-44 instance independence; NFR-01 per-UC `Containment`/`Observability` markers | — | — | **always** (read from the SPEC header / NFR file, not the profile) |
> Rows 1–3 are the auditor's two recommended overrides plus **SR-1**, which today's PIN established in practice: a defect class can outrank the profile, and when it does, the override must be written down rather than re-argued per incident.

`CLAUDE.md ## Rigor` names the profile but **defines no matrix** of what `prototype` turns off. Without one the profile is a label: the invariant *"every gate the profile turns off must be recorded"* cannot be applied mechanically, because nothing states which gates those are.

**The auditor's recommendation, worth taking:** write the matrix, **plus two per-path overrides**, because a project-wide profile that flattens the product's own gate to the same rigor as a docstring is the wrong shape for a product whose value is being a gate:
1. **The verdict path runs at `full` on every change** — `classifier/gate.py` (`overall_gate`) and `platform/scoring.py` (`build_score_inputs`'s gate value): fresh-instance stamp with mutation, never a skipped re-gate.
2. **A fix round that touches `src/` never qualifies for `re-gate skipped`, at any profile.** (F-1 was the first casualty of the missing rule, within hours of the profile change.)

## ~~DEBT-56~~ — ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-09-21 in `c449b65`** — `load_dotenv()` now delegates to `python-dotenv`

**Status:** ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-09-21** (orchestrator, in `c449b65`) · **Origin:** Dengoso `/implement` S-01.4 T-01.4.1, 2026-09-21 · **Impact: Low-Med**

> ⚠️ **Everything below this line described the state at the moment the batch shipped and is now HISTORICAL.** `python-dotenv>=1.2.3` **is** a declared dependency (`pyproject.toml`, locked in `uv.lock`), and `dotenv_support.py` delegates to it — the hand-rolled parser is deleted. **The row was left reading 'open / absent' inside the very commit that added the dependency**, which an independent review caught (R-5) along with the same false claim in `PROGRESS.md`, `HANDOFFS.md` and — worst — `tests/orchestration/test_dotenv_support.py`'s module docstring, which described the opposite of the file it tests.
> **How it was resolved:** the dependency was added and the workaround deleted, because a parser whose own docstring said *"not feature-complete — no `export` prefix, no `${VAR}` interpolation, no multiline values"* is a latent bug waiting for the first `.env` that uses one. **The implementer's handling was correct** — it hit a real scope wall (`pyproject.toml` belonged to a concurrent agent), shipped a tested workaround, and **disclosed it as debt instead of absorbing it silently**. That disclosure is why this got fixed within the hour.
> ⚠️ **The swap was not free, and that is the durable lesson:** it broke **nine tests** and exposed that a bare `load_dotenv()` resolves relative to **the calling frame's file** — the source tree — not the operator's cwd. The path is now pinned to `Path.cwd() / ".env"`. See `dotenv_support.py`'s docstring, which also records that the orchestrator's *first* explanation of that mechanism was itself wrong.

`python-dotenv` is not a declared runtime dependency (absent from `pyproject.toml`/`uv.lock`) even though ADR-0004 and every adapter/platform module's docstring already assume a `load_dotenv()` call site exists (`.env.example` line 4 states it as fact). T-01.4.1 is the first real call site (INV-05's "seam"), and the correct fix — add `python-dotenv` to `pyproject.toml` + regenerate `uv.lock` — is a `pyproject.toml` edit. This session's scope wall reserved `pyproject.toml` for the concurrent T-01.4.10 agent (credential hygiene / lock-file pin), so rather than touch that file, `idp_regression.orchestration.dotenv_support.load_dotenv()` reimplements the one behaviour this codebase relies on: read `KEY=VALUE` lines from a `.env` file in the cwd (if present), `os.environ.setdefault` per key, never override an already-set var. It is unit-tested (`tests/orchestration/test_dotenv_support.py`, 6 tests) and behaviorally equivalent for this codebase's actual `.env` format (confirmed against `.env.example`: no `export` prefix, no multiline values, no variable interpolation) — but it is not python-dotenv: no `export` prefix support, no multiline values, no `${VAR}` interpolation, no `.env`-file-path override, no `verbose`/`override` kwargs.

**Where (files):** `src/idp_regression/orchestration/dotenv_support.py`; call sites `src/idp_regression/orchestration/facade.py`, `src/idp_regression/orchestration/cli.py` (both import only the name `load_dotenv`, so swapping the implementation is a one-line, one-file change).

**Fix:** once `pyproject.toml` is free (after T-01.4.10 lands, or coordinated directly), add `python-dotenv` to `[project] dependencies`, regenerate `uv.lock` per `CLAUDE.md ## Tooling`, and replace `dotenv_support.py`'s body with `from dotenv import load_dotenv as load_dotenv` (or delete the module and repoint the two call sites at `dotenv` directly). Re-run `tests/orchestration/test_dotenv_support.py` against the real library (or delete it if the shim is removed) and `pip-audit`.

**Interest:** flat while `.env` files in this project stay single-line `KEY=VALUE` with no `export`/interpolation (true today, per `.env.example`); grows the day anyone reaches for a `.env` feature the shim doesn't support (e.g. `${IDP_REGION}`-style interpolation) — that would silently under-load rather than error, since a malformed line is skipped, not rejected.

## ~~DEBT-68 — `mypy src tests` has 27 errors; every agent has been running `mypy src` instead~~

**Status:** closed 2026-09-22 (Dengoso) · **Origin:** top-level verification of `6755fa1`, 2026-09-22 · **Impact: Medium**

**Resolution.** All 27 fixed, `mypy src tests` now 0 errors (80 source files). Breakdown:

- **19 were real test bugs**, fixed without touching what any test asserts: `tests/platform/test_scoring.py`'s `_EMPTY_GOLDEN` fixture (6 errors) was an untyped dict passed where `Golden` was expected — annotated it `Golden`. `tests/platform/test_langfuse_adapter.py`'s `_derived_score` helper (2 errors) returned `dict[str, Any]` when its literal shape is exactly `ScoreInput` — retyped it. `tests/orchestration/test_log_sanitize.py` (1 error) had `tmp_path`/`monkeypatch` fixture params typed `object` instead of `pathlib.Path`/`pytest.MonkeyPatch`, forcing three now-redundant ignores — retyped and dropped them. `tests/orchestration/test_facade.py:268` (1 error) carried a stale `# type: ignore[arg-type]` left over from before `_ExplodingLenList` was itself declared `# type: ignore[type-arg]` (making it `list[Any]`, so the assignment was already Any-compatible) — removed. Four `test_langfuse_adapter.py`/`test_make_platform_*` sites (isinstance-narrowing `adapter`/`http_client`/`sdk_client` from their Protocol types down to the concrete `LangfuseAdapter`/`UrllibHttpClient`, plus `assert sdk_client is not None`) — this *strengthens* those assertions (DoD (b): now verifies the real transport was wired, not just something Protocol-shaped), it doesn't touch what they assert about content. Two `test_record_run_preconditions.py` sites had the ignore's error code wrong (`typeddict-item` where mypy actually raised `list-item`) — corrected the code, not the assertion.
- **8 were deliberate-bad-input cases**, given a narrow coded ignore with a one-line comment, exactly per the "test deliberately feeds bad input" rule: `test_scoring.py`'s `gate=` params already had correct narrow ignores (untouched). `test_record_run_preconditions.py` — the mutated `bad_score` list-item (line ~657) and the non-list `scores` dict (moved the ignore onto the actual offending expression, corrected `typeddict-item`→its real code). `test_facade.py:1752` — see the `CancelledError` verdict below.
- **The 3 named SDK-private pins were NOT weakened** — `test_make_platform_dispatches_on_platform_env`, `test_make_platform_raises_when_langfuse_base_url_disagrees_with_langfuse_host` (this one had zero mypy errors to begin with — it uses a constructor spy, not private attrs), and `test_make_platform_constructs_the_sdk_client_with_base_url_equal_to_host` (also spy-based, zero errors). The attr access these pin — `sdk_client._base_url`, `sdk_client.api._client_wrapper.get_headers()` — is still read verbatim off the same live SDK object; the only change is (a) narrowing `adapter`/`sdk_client` away from their declared Protocol/`| None` types first via `isinstance`/`is not None` so mypy can see there's an object to access at all, then (b) a corrected, still-present `# type: ignore[attr-defined]` on the two lines that read the actual SDK privates, now with a comment naming why the ignore is deliberate. The pin still fires on the same two attribute paths; a version bump that removes them still breaks this test exactly as before.

**`CancelledError` verdict (`test_facade.py:1752`): production has no gap, the test's fixture was under-typed.** `asyncio.CancelledError` is a `BaseException`, not an `Exception` (confirmed: `facade.py:253/455/627` already catch `except (Exception, asyncio.CancelledError)` — the GAP-5 fix from `/harden` is already landed and does catch it at runtime; the test passes today). The only bug was `_RecordingPlatform.__init__`'s `mark_run_status_error` parameter being typed `Exception | None`, which can't accept the `CancelledError` the test deliberately raises to prove GAP-5's widened catch holds. Widened that one parameter to `BaseException | None` (test-double-only, not production) with a comment explaining why. `record_run_error` stayed `Exception | None` — nothing raises a `BaseException` through it.

**B-4 is what stops this recurring** — `.github/workflows/quality-gate.yml` now runs `mypy src tests` (the full, documented command) as a blocking CI job on every PR, so the narrower `mypy src` can no longer be silently substituted by habit the way it was before this row was filed.

**Verified:** `.venv/bin/mypy src tests` → 0 errors (80 files). `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` → 886 passed, 15 skipped (unchanged from `6755fa1`). `.venv/bin/ruff check src tests` → clean. `.venv/bin/pip-audit` → no known vulnerabilities.

`CLAUDE.md ## Commands` documents the type gate as `.venv/bin/mypy src tests` (strict). `mypy src` alone is clean — `mypy src tests` reports **27 errors in 5 files**, all under `tests/`:

| File | Errors |
|---|---|
| `tests/platform/test_langfuse_adapter.py` | 15 |
| `tests/platform/test_record_run_preconditions.py` | 6 |
| `tests/platform/test_scoring.py` | 6 |
| `tests/orchestration/test_facade.py` | 2 (incl. an `unused-ignore` and a `CancelledError` passed where `Exception \| None` is expected) |
| `tests/orchestration/test_log_sanitize.py` | 1 |

**Pre-existing, not introduced by `6755fa1`** — that commit touched only `adapter/` and `tests/adapter/`, and no error is in either. It went unnoticed because every `/implement` and `/test` round in this project has verified with `mypy src`, which is the narrower command.

**Why it matters beyond tidiness.** The errors are concentrated in the platform suite — the tests that pin the Langfuse SDK's *private* surfaces (`_base_url`, `api._client_wrapper.get_headers()`), which `CLAUDE.md ## External services` already names as "the first thing a version bump breaks silently". A type checker that is never run over those files is one fewer signal on exactly the surface flagged as most likely to break quietly. It also compounds `/signoff` blocker **B-4** (CI runs no tests and no type-check at all), so nothing catches this downstream either.

**Fix:** run `mypy src tests` and resolve the 27, then make it the command every agent runs. The durable fix is B-4 — put the real gate in CI so the narrower command cannot be substituted by habit.

**Interest:** grows with each new platform test written under a checker that never looks at it, and at the next Langfuse version bump.

## ~~DEBT-84~~ — `JobRegistry.is_busy()` briefly holds the exclusive lock it is probing — ✅ **CLOSED 2026-09-25**

> ✅ **CLOSED (Lane F).** See the Lanes D & F section above. Two corrections to this row, both
> found by measuring rather than reasoning: the contention is **`is_busy()`-vs-`start()`**, not
> start-vs-start (the UI *polls* `is_busy()`), at **64 spurious 409s per 5000 acquires (~1.3%)**;
> and **the fix prescribed below does not close it** — when a job runs locally `start()` would
> correctly fail anyway, and when none runs (the spurious case) `is_busy()` still probes. Fixed
> instead with an opt-in bounded retry only `start()` passes: a probe holds the lock for
> microseconds, a real job for minutes. Measured **0 per 5000**, genuine conflict still refused in
> ~0.26s. Pinned by `tests/ui/test_workspace_lock_race.py`; mutants for the budget (×100, 1e-6,
> ignored, default-changed) and for `is_busy()` all redden.
>
> **Original row preserved below, including its wrong prescription**, because the reasoning error
> is the useful part: it was derived from the clause shape rather than from a measurement.

**Found:** 2026-09-25, Zangado QA final round on Epic E tickets 5–7.
**Where:** `src/idp_regression/ui/jobs.py::JobRegistry.is_busy` (and `_WorkspaceLock`).

**What.** `is_busy()` answers "does a quota-spending job hold this workspace?" by *taking* the `flock` and immediately releasing it. Probing the lock (rather than this process's own job table) is deliberate and correct — another console on the same workspace holds it just as effectively, and a UI that only knew about its own jobs would offer a Run button that is about to 409. But for the microseconds it holds the lock, a genuine concurrent `start()` sees the workspace as busy and is refused with a 409.

**Why it is debt rather than a bug.** It **fails safe in the only direction that matters**: the refusal costs nothing, no quota is spent, and a retry succeeds. The opposite error — a probe that reported "free" while a batch was running — would let two batches interleave against the same pin store, which is the whole reason the lock exists.

**Why it is not fixed now.** `flock` has no test-without-acquire. The primitive that does (`fcntl.lockf` + `F_GETLK`) carries a worse footgun: POSIX record locks are dropped when *any* file descriptor for the file is closed in the process, so a single unrelated `open`/`close` elsewhere in the console would silently release a live job's lock. Trading a harmless spurious 409 for a silent lock release on a quota-spending job is the wrong trade.

**Fix, when it is worth it:** have `is_busy()` consult a registry-local "a job is running here" flag first and only probe the lock when that flag is clear, which removes the self-contention for the common case (this console's own job) while keeping the cross-console answer.

**Interest:** flat. Only reachable when two `start()` calls race within microseconds, on a console that admits one job at a time by design.
